# Run notes

Every row on the board, with the note and caveat it carries. Generated from `data/benchmark.json` by `bench_golive.py` - do not edit by hand.

Updated Sep 23, 2026 · 143 rows from 220 scored runs. Superseded rows are listed last.

## GPT-6 Astra (max effort) - mean of 3

**45 / 105** · repo 1 22/45 · repo 2 23/60 · 55 unplanted · 89.8 min · $33.03 · `max` · Codex CLI · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-14

**Caveat.** Every figure on this row is a MEAN of three runs, carried to one decimal - 45.0 of 105. The three runs scored 48, 40 and 47: a range of 8, which is the widest spread measured on any row above 30 here and is wider than the gap between this row and the next. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run max row (48/105) published 2026-09-04, which was the best of the three; the supersession is about method, not a correction, and that receipt stands. READ THE TOP OF THIS BOARD ACCORDINGLY: the leader's own spread is larger than the distance to the rows beneath it, so the ordering at the top is not something three runs can establish. The rows it is being compared against are still single runs with spreads nobody has measured.

THE TOP ROW OF THIS BOARD, RUN THREE TIMES. Runs scored 48, 40 and 47 of 105 (repo 1: 24, 21, 21 - repo 2: 24, 19, 26). Mean 45.0, range 8. *** WHY THIS ARM MATTERED MOST *** Every other row is read against the leader, so the leader was the single run whose spread was worth the most and known the least. It is still the leader: 45.0 is the highest figure on the board and no other row is close to it at n=3. What changed is the width. *** THE RANGE IS 8 POINTS, AND IT IS NOT A SMALL NUMBER HERE *** Eight points of 105 is wider than the gap between the leader and the row below it, wider than most adjacent pairs anywhere on this board, and it sits on a configuration nothing about which was varied: same model, prompt, harness, route, effort flag and judge. The single run published on 2026-09-04 scored 48, which is the TOP of its own three-run range, so every comparison drawn against 48 was drawn against this configuration's best day rather than its typical one. That is not a correction to the old row - 48 happened, and its receipt stands - it is a correction to what the number could be used for. *** WHAT IT DOES AND DOES NOT MOVE *** It does not change the ORDER at the top: 45.0 still leads, and the gap to the next row is now 2 points rather than 5. It does mean that gap is inside the leader's own measured spread, so 'Astra max beats the row below it' is no longer something this board can assert - the row below is itself a single run with an unmeasured spread. Two numbers of unknown width two points apart support no ordering at all. *** THE UNPLANTED COLUMN MOVED FAR MORE THAN THE SCORE *** Genuine extras came in at 45, 58 and 62 across the three runs, a range of 17 on a count that is not scored. The score varied by 8 while the unscored work varied by 17, which is worth knowing before anyone reads a single run's extras figure as a property of a model. All six legs were clean: every leg exited 0, none carries a handicap note, and zero claimed-only fixes were recorded across all six - this arm claimed nothing the judge did not confirm, on any run. Per-leg receipts (repo 1 / repo 2): wall 37.2/41.6, 51.3/44.7 and 35.9/58.7 minutes; cost 15.07/16.14, 17.59/16.14 and 12.33/21.82 dollars. Run 3 ran 20 per cent longer than run 1 and scored one point lower. *** ONE JUDGING NOTE, BECAUSE IT NEARLY BECAME A SILENT DIFFERENCE *** Run 3's repo-2 leg created a scratch directory for itself, holding 300 copies of the files it was about to edit. Every copy was verified byte-identical to the pristine repo, so it contained no fix and no evidence, but left in the diff it would have added two megabytes to that leg's judge packet - a packet neither of the other two runs had. It is excluded, so all three runs were judged on the same shape of evidence. The alternative repair, raising the size limit, would have judged one replicate on a materially different packet from its own siblings. Each leg is a full independent run of both repos; the mean is over three, and the per-repo runs are stated so the spread stays visible - it is the finding, not a caveat on it. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: token counts at published list rates.

## GPT-5.6 Sol (max effort) - mean of 2

**43.5 / 105** · repo 1 19/45 · repo 2 24.5/60 · 48.5 unplanted · 253.1 min · $95.35 · `max` · Codex CLI · mean of 2 runs · 2026-09-16

**Caveat.** TWO RUNS IS TWO POINTS. The range between them is 7 of 105, which is wider than the gap between several adjacent rows on this board, so treat 43.5 as an estimate with a known spread rather than a settled number - and note the repo figures round independently of the combined mean, so they can sum to 0.1 away from it. The replication is UNVERIFIED: this harness reports no context accounting, so nothing confirms the two runs shared a configuration beyond their identical arm definitions. A third run was attempted and did not finish.

THE FIRST REPLICATION OF THIS MODEL'S TOP EFFORT SETTING. Runs scored 40 and 47 of 105 (repo 1: 18, 20 - repo 2: 22, 27). Mean 43.5, range 7. *** WHY THIS ROW IS n=2 AND NOT n=3 *** A third run was attempted on 2026-09-16 and did not complete: it ran 156 minutes, finished 563 tool calls, and then the harness account's usage quota was exhausted mid-turn. It produced no report and recorded no tokens and no cost, so there is nothing to score and nothing to average. It is not on this board and is not counted as a low run - a leg that never finished is not a result. Re-running it was declined on cost. *** THE SINGLE-RUN ROW ABOVE SAYS 42 AND THIS MEAN USES 40 FOR THE SAME RUN. BOTH ARE REAL. *** Run 1's transcript has been judged twice. The 2026-07-31 pass scored 19 + 23 = 42 and recorded its judge as grok-4.5 with no served-model field at all, because that column did not exist yet. The 2026-09-13 re-judge scored 18 + 22 = 40 and recorded the model the server actually served, grok-4.6. The judge bridge's model flag is inert - a measurement from 2026-09-04, where grok-4.5, grok-4.6 and a deliberately nonexistent model string all came back as grok-4.6 - so the July pass recorded a REQUEST as though it were a fact and was in all likelihood served the same judge. This mean therefore uses 40: the pass whose judge identity was MEASURED, and the same pass configuration run 2 was scored under. That makes it comparable and documented, NOT more accurate, and the older row keeps its 42 as a receipt rather than being quietly rewritten. *** WHAT THE TWO PASSES BUY, WHICH IS AN ERROR BAR AND NOT SAMPLE SIZE *** They disagree on 3 bugs of 105: on repo 1 one bug moved from a full fix to a partial, on repo 2 two credited bugs became misses. So JUDGE variance on a fixed transcript is 2 points, against MODEL variance of 7 points between the two runs - run-to-run spread is roughly three and a half times the grading noise. Two gradings of one transcript are NOT two runs and were not treated as such; n counts runs of the model. *** THE TWO RUNS COST VERY DIFFERENT AMOUNTS FOR A 7-POINT GAIN *** Run 2 consumed 74 percent more tokens than run 1 (100.9M against 176.0M) and 74 percent more at list rates (69.61 against 121.09 dollars) to score 7 points higher. The runs are six weeks apart on a self-updating CLI that records no version in its transcripts, so whether that is the same model working harder or a changed serving path is NOT established here. Read the mean as a mean of two runs that differed in effort expended as well as in outcome. *** THE REGIME BEHIND THESE TWO RUNS IS UNVERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED *** The automated regime check reads context accounting out of transcripts; this harness reports none - no init record and zero input tokens on every turn - so it returns CANNOT TELL for both repos rather than confirming the runs match. Stated plainly because the alternative is implying a check that did not happen. *** WALL TIME IS NOT COMPARABLE ACROSS THE TWO RUNS *** Run 1's legs ran alone; both of run 2's legs shared the machine with other work and say so in their own rows. Contention can inflate a wall figure and cannot shrink it, so the wall mean here is an upper bound. Scores, tokens and cost are unaffected by it. *** WHAT IS SOLID *** Counts, not inferences. A fixed configuration produced 40 and 47 of 105. Run 2 needed five attempts to produce two clean legs - three earlier legs died on wall budgets or non-zero exits and one was orphaned - so the failure rate of this configuration on this hardware is itself part of the picture and is visible in the metrics receipts.

## Opus 5.5 (max effort) - mean of 3

**41.7 / 105** · repo 1 19/45 · repo 2 22.7/60 · 9 unplanted · 66.9 min · $58.53 · `max` · Claude Code · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-23

**Caveat.** Three runs of an identical configuration scoring 43, 39 and 43 of 105 - spread 4. Read the mean with that width, not as a point. Runs a 1M context window with zero compactions; every Opus 5 row here runs 200K and compacted, so this ladder does not compare cleanly to that one. Cost is a list-rate estimate, not an invoice. Wall figures are contended.

Anthropic's Opus 5.5 at max effort through Claude Code on the first-party API, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 43, 39 and 43 of 105 (repo 1: 18, 17, 22 of 45 - repo 2: 25, 22, 21 of 60). Mean 41.7, spread 4. *** THIS SUPERSEDES OPUS 5.5 (MAX EFFORT) - MEAN OF 2 (41.0 OF 105) *** *** WHY THE REPLICATES EXIST *** the first pass measured this ladder at one run per rung and it came out monotone - medium 30, high 32, xhigh 35, max 43. That was suggestive and nothing more: no gap in it exceeded the 13-point spread this board has measured between two runs of ONE identical max-effort configuration, so the ordering of any two adjacent rungs was unresolved and every note said so. Three runs per rung is what can resolve it, and the whole ladder is being re-measured that way rather than only its top. The board currently reads low 22.3 (n=3), medium 30.3 (n=3), high 31.7 (n=3), xhigh 36 (n=3), max 41.7 (n=3). *** THE REPLICATES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE FIRST RUN *** same model string claude-opus-5-5[1m], same effort, same timeout, same price table, same blind judge. Only the arm name differs, so the spread above is run-to-run variance and nothing else. *** WALL AND COST *** Walls 68.5, 60.0, 72.2 minutes, mean 66.9; cost $60.49, $58.39, $56.69, mean $58.52. COST IS A LIST ESTIMATE at Anthropic's published Opus 5.5 rates - $4 input, $20 output, $0.20 cache read (a 0.05x cache multiplier unique to this model, where every other model here uses 0.1x) and $8 cache write, the 1-hour rate, which is what this harness actually writes and what the streams confirm. Not an invoice, hence `list`. *** GENUINE EXTRAS *** 9, 9 and 9 across the runs, mean 9.0 - real defects found in the repos that nobody planted, reported separately and never folded into the score. *** THE CONTEXT WINDOW *** every run here holds a 1M window and crosses zero compaction boundaries, so these rows compare cleanly to each other and NOT to the Opus 5 ladder, which ran the CLI's default 200K and compacted repeatedly mid-task. Autocompact ran at the CLI's own default throughout. *** RECEIPTS *** All legs exited 0, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on every leg - an OpenAI model scoring an Anthropic one, so neither the model itself nor a sibling of it. The runs shared the laptop with legs from other vendors, which the wall marker discloses; scores, tokens and cost are untouched by that, only the clock.

## Muse Spark 1.3 (max effort, Muse Code, Meta API) - mean of 5

**32.2 / 105** · repo 1 14.2/45 · repo 2 18/60 · 24 unplanted · 85.6 min · $18.11 · `max` · Muse Code / Meta API · mean of 5 runs · 2026-09-17

**Caveat.** FIVE RUNS, RANGE 6. The spread is the finding, not the mean: at n=3 this arm looked tight (range 2), and two more runs tripled the error bar. Treat 32.2 as an estimate with a wide known spread. TWO OF THE FIVE RUNS ARE SPLIT-VERSION - the harness updated itself mid-experiment and their repo-2 legs ran a newer build; the row explains why the evidence says that is not what lowered them. The replication is UNVERIFIED: this harness reports no context accounting, so nothing confirms the five runs shared a configuration beyond identical arm definitions. Repo figures round independently of the combined mean and can sum to 0.1 away from it.

THE DEEPEST REPLICATION ON THIS BOARD: FIVE RUNS OF ONE CONFIGURATION. Runs scored 33, 33, 35, 29 and 31 of 105 (repo 1: 14, 17, 15, 12, 13 - repo 2: 19, 16, 20, 17, 18). Mean 32.2, range 6. *** WHAT THE TWO EXTRA RUNS BOUGHT WAS AN ERROR BAR, NOT A SCORE. *** The mean moved 1.5 points, from 33.7 to 32.2. The RANGE went from 2 to 6. At n=3 this arm read as tight and well-behaved - 33, 33, 35. At n=5 it spans 29 to 35, which is wider than the gap between several adjacent rows here. The three-run version did not merely estimate the mean imprecisely; it understated how much this configuration varies, in the direction that made it look steadier than it is. Read 32.2 as an estimate with a known and fairly wide spread. *** THE HARNESS UPDATED ITSELF DURING THIS EXPERIMENT, AND TWO RUNS ARE SPLIT-VERSION. *** Muse Code polls a release channel and replaces its own binary. Run 1 ran Muse Code 1.1.1; runs 2-5 ran 1.2.1; and the repo-2 legs of runs 4 and 5 came up on 1.3.0. So those two runs were graded across two harness versions. There is no version pin in the launcher, the channel serves only the current release, and the previous binary is deleted on upgrade, so 1.2.1 cannot be restored and this is not repairable by re-running. Further drift is now suppressed. *** THE DRIFT IS NOT WHAT LOWERED THE SCORE, AND THE SPLIT IS WHAT SHOWS IT. *** The obvious worry is that runs 4 and 5 are low because 1.3.0 is worse. Two facts say otherwise. First, the drop lives on repo 1, where the harness never changed: all four of runs 2-5 ran 1.2.1 there and scored 17, 15, 12 and 13 - a spread of 5 on a single repo with the version held constant. Second, repo 2 shows no version effect at all: the 1.2.1 runs scored 16 and 20, and the two 1.3.0 runs scored 17 and 18, sitting inside that range rather than below it. The low runs are run-to-run variance. That the drift landed on the half where it cannot be doing the damage is luck, not design, and it is disclosed here rather than quietly dropped because the answer happened to be convenient. *** THE REGIME BEHIND THESE RUNS IS UNVERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED. *** CANNOT TELL (gbvb); CANNOT TELL (abhb) - the automated check reads context accounting out of transcripts and this harness reports none, so it cannot confirm the five runs shared a regime. Stated plainly because the alternative is implying a check that did not happen. *** WALL TIME IS AN UPPER BOUND AND IS NOT COMPARABLE ACROSS THE FIVE RUNS. *** Runs 1-3 ran their legs alone. Runs 4 and 5 were run concurrently with each other, a deliberate exception to the usual one-leg-per-provider rule, and their repo-2 legs also shared the machine with other work; every affected row says so itself. Contention can inflate a wall figure and cannot shrink it. Scores, tokens and cost are unaffected. *** THE FAILURE RATE IS PART OF THE PICTURE AND IS VISIBLE IN THE RECEIPTS. *** Runs 4 and 5 needed several attempts on repo 2. Three attempts died without producing a report because the account's subscription quota was exhausted mid-leg - the retry layer absorbed the rate-limit responses and the harness reported three different and all misleading causes, including two stream-idle timeouts and a suspected local network fault. Those attempts produced no report and no score, are NOT counted as low runs here, and cost 8.35 dollars between them. A leg that never finished is not a result. The five runs that did finish cost 90.55 dollars in total.

## GPT-5.6 Luna (max effort) - mean of 3

**31.3 / 105** · repo 1 16/45 · repo 2 15.3/60 · 54.7 unplanted · 187.1 min · $3.15 · `max` · Codex CLI · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-22

**Caveat.** Three runs of an identical configuration spanning 30 to 33 of 105 - read the mean with that width in mind, not as a point. Cost is a TOKEN ESTIMATE at list rates, not a bill, and is comparable only to other list-priced rows. Runs shared the laptop with benchmark legs from other vendors, so wall figures are contended and biased upward; scores and cost are unaffected.

GPT-5.6 Luna at max effort through OpenAI's own Codex CLI, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 33, 30 and 31 of 105 (repo 1: 17, 16, 15 of 45 - repo 2: 16, 14, 16 of 60). Mean 31.3, spread 3. *** THIS SUPERSEDES THE MEAN OF TWO (31.5 of 105) *** a third independent run - held back earlier today by a Grok judge-bridge fault unrelated to Luna itself - completes the originally planned n=3. The mean moved from 31.5 to 31.3; the three runs span 30 to 33, and that span is the honest width of this measurement. *** WALL AND COST *** Walls 85.8, 254.3, 221.5 minutes, mean 187.2; cost $1.80, $3.85, $3.80, mean $3.15. COST IS A TOKEN ESTIMATE at OpenAI's published rates, not an invoice - the Codex CLI on a subscription does not expose a per-run bill, so this column is `list` and comparable only to other list rows. *** GENUINE EXTRAS *** 31, 57 and 76 across the three runs, mean 54.7 - real defects found in the repos that are not part of the planted set, rising noticeably across the replicates; reported because a model that finds bugs nobody planted is doing something the planted total cannot see, not folded into the scored total. *** THE TIER IS FIRST-PARTY AND THAT IS WHY IT CAN BE ASSERTED *** max is Luna's top tier on OpenAI's own endpoint, which validates and separates its effort levels rather than silently clamping an unknown string the way several third-party routes on this board do. *** THE JUDGE IS NOT THIS BOARD'S DEFAULT, BY RULE *** A model may never judge itself or a sibling, and this is an OpenAI model, so the default Codex judge is disqualified - and so is any other first-party GPT-5.6 checkpoint on this board (e.g. Sol, Terra), since the rule is about shared weights, not identity-knowledge alone. Four legs were judged by grok-4.7 and two by grok-4.5 (the id is the one the scoring receipt records as SERVED, not the one the judge config asked for - from 2026-09-22 the endpoint has been resolving a request for grok-4.5 to grok-4.7, and notes published before 2026-09-23 named the requested id), blinded to the arm's identity. The runs themselves shared the machine with legs from other vendors, which the row's wall marker discloses; scores, tokens and cost are untouched by that - only the clock is. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL within every run.

## Union Alpha (stealth; free) - mean of 3

**30.7 / 105** · repo 1 16/45 · repo 2 14.7/60 · 10.3 unplanted · 38.2 min · $4.81 · `default` · Claude Code / OpenRouter · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-17

Runs scored 32, 34 and 26 of 105 (repo 1: 16, 19, 13 - repo 2: 16, 15, 13). Mean 30.7, range 8. *** THE SPREAD IS THE FINDING, AND IT IS WIDE. *** Three runs of one fixed ARM DEFINITION on what was then a free stealth slug, revealed 2026-09-17 as unbiased/pareto - a COMPOSITE that runs several models in parallel per request and selects or synthesises the output. Read every number here as a blend's, not a model's. The published single run sat in the middle of a range that no single run could have shown. Read the mean as an estimate with a known and fairly wide spread, not as a score. *** ONE MEMBER RAN ITSELF INTO THE CONTEXT CEILING AND THE OTHER TWO DID NOT. *** r3's repo-1 leg peaked at 197,452 tokens against this endpoint's 262,144 ceiling and was compacted once by the harness; r1 peaked at 23,028 and neither r1 nor r2 compacted at all. Every configuration parameter was held fixed - same slug, same single endpoint, same harness build 2.1.274, same prompt, same repos, same judge - so what differed is what the MODEL did: r3 made about 2.3x the tokens and 1.9x the wall of r1. It is averaged in rather than dropped, because dropping a member for behaving differently selects runs on their behaviour. The automated regime check returns CANNOT TELL on this arm (r2's transcript reports no context accounting), so this is stated from the gateway log rather than implied by a check that did not run. The replication is therefore UNVERIFIED rather than verified: beyond identical arm definitions and the gateway log, nothing confirms the three runs shared a regime. *** WHAT THESE RUNS MEASURED WAS ZERO, AND ONE FIGURE BEHIND THAT WAS REPAIRED. *** The slug was priced at $0/$0 in preview and billed nothing: a live call through this arm's own pinned path returned usage.cost 0, upstream_inference_cost 0, and a credits delta of exactly $0.000000. r3's two legs nonetheless recorded 0.0787 and 0.0662, because for aggregator arms the runner measures a CREDITS DELTA across the leg - so any other consumer on the same account lands in it, and another session was fingerprinting this model against PAID reference models through r3's window. Both cells were repaired to 0 and both originals are preserved in the metrics receipts. r1 and r2 measured 0 unaided. WHAT THESE RUNS WOULD COST AT THE REVEALED PRICE: $14.43 for all three, $4.81 per run, repricing their own measured token counts at unbiased/pareto list ($2.50 prompt, $7.50 completion, $0.25 cached read per million). So the cell reads $4.81 and is marked cost_list, which the glossary defines as a token-count estimate at published list rates and not a bill. It was $0.00 until the preview ended; an honest zero then stopped supporting the only comparison the column exists for, so the figure was repriced generator-side. Nothing was actually spent, and the published receipts still say so. *** THE SLUG SERVED MORE THAN ONE VOCABULARY. *** Predicted before the reveal and confirmed against the revealed endpoint: at least three backends answer to this slug. The benchmark runs themselves do NOT look mixed: the three replicates agree at mean Jaccard 0.628 on their fixed-bug sets against a board median of 0.632 over 24 replicate families, which is ordinary. The probe evidence suggests the router was length-sensitive, so short probes and long agentic runs need not have landed on the same backend. The score is what was measured; that it describes a single model is not something this board can claim. *** THE JUDGE IS NOT THIS BOARD'S DEFAULT. *** A model may not judge itself or a sibling, and at run time the lab was unknown, so the scoring pass ran on grok-4.6 rather than the board default. The reveal makes that the right call for a firmer reason than the one recorded: a COMPOSITE has no single vendor to check a judge against, and third-party analysis of the panel places GPT-6 Astra on the escalation path - the default judge's own family, inside the thing being judged. All three runs were scored by grok-4.6. r1 was ALSO judged by codex into a separate scratch pass, and the two judges selected the SAME 32 bugs - identical sets on both repos, not merely equal counts. *** NO EFFORT DIAL EXISTS ON THIS ROUTE: supported_parameters carries no reasoning field, the model returns reasoning_tokens=0, and an invented tier is refused by the gateway in 0.2s without reaching the provider. So effort is 'default' and deliberately not 'inert_default' - nothing was asserted, and there was no dial to probe.

COST IS A LIST ESTIMATE at Anthropic's published Opus 5.5 rates - $4 input, $20 output, $0.20 cache read (a 0.05x cache multiplier unique to this model, where every other model here uses 0.1x) and $8 cache write, the 1-hour rate, which is what this harness actually writes and what the streams confirm. Not an invoice, hence `list`. *** GENUINE EXTRAS *** 15, 13 and 12 across the runs, mean 13.3 - real defects found in the repos that nobody planted, reported separately and never folded into the score. *** THE CONTEXT WINDOW *** every run here holds a 1M window and crosses zero compaction boundaries, so these rows compare cleanly to each other and NOT to the Opus 5 ladder, which ran the CLI's default 200K and compacted repeatedly mid-task. Autocompact ran at the CLI's own default throughout. *** RECEIPTS *** All legs exited 0, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on every leg - an OpenAI model scoring an Anthropic one, so neither the model itself nor a sibling of it. This row lands at 29.3, below all three, at a fraction of the cost. The single run that first showed this was one draw on a board that has measured a 13-point spread between two runs of one identical max-effort configuration - which is exactly why it was replicated rather than left to stand. *** THE REPLICATES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE FIRST RUN *** same model string gpt-6-sol, same effort, same timeout, same pinned executable, same price table, same blind judge. Extras are reported separately and never folded into the score, but a model that flags this many unplanted defects while fixing fewer planted ones is spending its attention somewhere the planted total cannot see. *** IT KILLED AN ALL-TIME SURVIVOR *** the first run fixed a planted bug that no scored pass by any model had ever fixed before - a settings-persistence ordering defect where the stored value is written before the live switch it is supposed to describe has landed. That is a first for this row and it stands regardless of where the mean settles. *** THIS ROW RUNS A DIFFERENT CLI BUILD FROM EVERY OTHER OPENAI ROW, UNAVOIDABLY *** the Codex build the rest of this board uses (0.154.0) refuses this model outright - 'The gpt-6-sol model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account'. 0.155.1 runs it on the same account, same auth, same machine, so the blocker was the client and not entitlement. Legs were judged by grok-4.7 with a single exception - repo 1 of the second run drew grok-4.5 - blinded to the arm's identity. *** RECEIPTS *** All legs exited 0 with a report written, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. **Caveat.** One run per tier, five tiers - so this is five samples describing a surface, not a measured response curve. The board's own variance evidence (three runs of one model at one setting scoring 16, 13 and 17) means a twelve-point spread across four single runs is entirely consistent with noise, which is precisely the point: if noise can produce this ordering, the ordering carries no information. The one claim that survives is max against the rest, and even that rests on a single run at each end.

The row that completes Fable 5.1's dial, and the one that shows the dial does not behave. All five tiers now exist at one run each: low 29, medium 21, high 33, xhigh 29, max 43. The four below max span twelve points in no order whatsoever - xhigh lands BELOW high and exactly level with low, medium is the worst of the five - while max sits ten clear above the best of them. The run itself is clean: both legs exited success with a report written, 13/45 on repo 1 and 16/60 on repo 2. So the honest reading of this model's effort control is that only the top of it is doing visible work on this benchmark; between low and xhigh the label does not predict the score. That matters because the effort column is the thing readers most often take as an ordering, and here it is not one.

## Grok 4.7 (xhigh) seq - mean of 4

**28.8 / 105** · repo 1 13/45 · repo 2 15.8/60 · 31.8 unplanted · 46.0 min · $22.89 floor · `xhigh` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · mean of 4 runs · 2026-09-21

**Caveat.** A mean of FOUR runs against a 4.6 row that is a mean of three - the samples are not the same size. The four span 25 to 31 of 105 against 4.6's 27 to 30, so the tie still rests on the wider distribution. Cost uses a per-session reconstruction the other grok rows do not yet use; the note gives both figures and the gap holds either way. Two of the four runs share a machine with each other, so only the wall figures from runs 1 and 4 support a speed comparison. The runs spawned different numbers of subagent sessions on identical arm definitions, which the board's regime check cannot verify on this harness.

Grok 4.7 at xhigh through xAI's own CLI, published as the MEAN OF FOUR INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 25, 30, 31 and 29 of 105 (repo 1: 12, 12, 16, 12 - repo 2: 13, 18, 15, 17). Mean 28.8, range 6. *** THE HEADLINE IS STILL THAT NOTHING MOVED *** Grok 4.6 at the SAME tier, a mean of three, stands at 28.7 of 105. This row is 28.8. The fourth run was added specifically to test whether the tie was an artefact of three draws; it was not - the headline moved by +0.1 of a bug. This supersedes the mean-of-3 row published earlier the same day, which said the same thing on less evidence. A tie is a measurement, and publishing only when a number improves would make this a scoreboard for vendors rather than a benchmark. *** THE IDENTICAL TOTAL HIDES A SWAP, AND ONE REPO IS NEARLY CONSTANT *** Repo 1 scored 12, 12, 16, 12 of 45 against 4.6's mean of 11.7; repo 2 scored 13, 18, 15, 17 of 60 against 17.0. Three of the four repo-1 runs landed on the same number, which makes the fourth look like the outlier rather than the ceiling; repo 2 scatters across five points. Both movements sit inside the spread of the runs producing them. *** THE SPEED QUESTION IS SETTLED, AND THE ANSWER IS NO *** Runs 2 and 3 overlapped each other on one machine, so their walls are biased upward and the earlier row published this comparison as undecidable. Runs 1 and 4 each had the machine to themselves: 39.6 and 45.4 minutes, a mean of 42.5 against 43.1 for the three 4.6 runs. That is a difference of under two minutes on runs that take three quarters of an hour. Any earlier reading that this generation is faster came from a single run and does not survive a second clean one. *** WHAT DOES MOVE, AND IT MOVES THE WRONG WAY: COST *** Reconstructed floors of $16.54, $23.04, $29.94, $22.01 for a mean of $22.88, against $16.89 for the three 4.6 runs on the SAME arithmetic - about 35% more for the same score. READ THE COST COLUMN CAREFULLY: the reconstruction used to take the largest context in a run and charge every request in that run against it, which overstates any run that fans out into subagents by up to 25%, and it now measures each session separately. This row carries the corrected figure; the 4.6 row still shows $18.60 on the old arithmetic and has not yet been restated. On that same old arithmetic these four runs come to $27.10. The gap is about a third either way - what is inconsistent across the board right now is the arithmetic, not the finding. Both are floors twice over: the grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates on the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis, and xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates, which these runs did, by an amount this method cannot see. *** AND WHAT MOVES IN 4.7'S FAVOUR: BUGS NOBODY PLANTED *** Genuine extras - real defects in the repo that are not part of the planted set - ran 26, 35, 34 and 32 across the four runs, a mean of 31.8, against 18.0 for 4.6: roughly 76% more, and the most stable difference between the two models in this whole wave. The most defensible reading is that 4.7 finds materially more real bugs than 4.6 and no more of the ones being counted. Whether that is a better bug-hunter or a worse instruction-follower is a judgment the number does not settle. *** PER BUG, THE TWO MODELS ARE NEARLY THE SAME MODEL *** Measured across the first three runs a side: on repo 1 each touched 18 distinct planted bugs with 15 shared; on repo 2, 21 against 22 with 18 shared. There is not one bug on either repo that 4.6 found on all three runs and 4.7 never found, and exactly one the other way. Every other bug unique to one model was a single-run hit - noise, not capability. *** THE RUNS DID NOT BEHAVE ALIKE, AND THE AUTOMATIC CHECK CANNOT SEE IT HERE *** The regime check that refuses a mean over mismatched configurations parses Claude Code transcripts, so it returns NOT CHECKED for every grok row on this board. The CLI's own session dirs show what it would look for: sessions spawned per leg (repo 1 / repo 2) ran run 1 1 and 1; run 2 1 and 3; run 3 5 and 1; run 4 2 and 5. Grok 4.6 never once ran a leg as a single session across its six; this model did it four times out of eight, and when it does fan out it commits far harder - up to 206 inference requests against 4.6's 88 to 128. Across these four runs fan-out tracks COST almost mechanically, and what it does to SCORE is unresolved rather than nothing: ordered by inference requests the four runs scored 25, 30, 29 and 31, so the heaviest fan-out landed mid-pack but the run with NO fan-out landed LAST, not mid-pack. The rank correlation against score is positive and four runs cannot separate that from chance. CORRECTED 2026-09-21: an earlier version of this note said the no-fan-out run landed mid-pack and that fan-out tracked score not at all; the first half was simply wrong - that run scored lowest of the four - and the second was stated more firmly than four runs support. The arm definitions are byte-identical clones, so this is emergent agent behaviour rather than configuration. An OBSERVATION over four runs, not a mechanism. *** THE TIER IS THE MODEL CEILING AND THAT WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED *** A zero-token ACP readback before each launch returned reasoning_effort xhigh for a requested xhigh, and the enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh with high as the CLI default - nothing above xhigh exists to ask for. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string, so an echo is the only thing that makes this label honest. The served model is named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. *** RECEIPTS *** All eight legs exited 0. Per-run wall 39.6, 47.3, 51.8, 45.4 minutes; runs 1 and 4 uncontended, runs 2 and 3 overlapping each other. Legs SEQUENTIAL within every run, one repo at a time - that is what seq means here. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on all eight legs, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 27 to 30. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run sequential row (27/105) published 2026-08-28, which was the LOWEST of the three. Mean 28.7, range 3. *** WHAT THIS CORRECTS IS AN INFERENCE, NOT A NUMBER *** The single run this replaces scored 27, and its note read that as settled because the 2026-08-12 concurrent-legs run had also scored 27 - 'n=2 agreement'. Two further sequential runs came in at 30 and 29. The 27 was correct for the run that produced it; what does not survive is treating a pair of agreeing draws as a measurement of the rung. Both 27s sit at the bottom of the range this configuration actually spans. *** WHERE THE MOVEMENT IS, AND WHAT THAT IS WORTH *** The three totals span 3 points, but the per-repo legs span more: repo 1 scored 8, 14 and 13 of 45, repo 2 scored 19, 16 and 16 of 60. The repo-1 range alone is 6 points on a 45-bug repo, wider than the range of the totals it feeds. The legs moved in opposite directions and the totals landed close together. That is an OBSERVATION about three runs and nothing more - it is not a mechanism, and it is not a claim that totals are steadier than legs. Three draws cannot establish that, and this pattern arises from chance often enough to be unremarkable. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Per-leg (repo 1 / repo 2): wall 19.1/22.0, 18.8/23.5 and 23.9/21.9 minutes; floor 6.84/10.12, 9.38/8.59 and 13.57/7.30 dollars. Genuine extras 16, 16 and 22. Claimed-only 1, 0 and 1, all on repo 1. COST IS A FLOOR, NOT A BILL: the grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row on this board is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates. The three floors are 16.96, 17.96 and 20.88 dollars and this row shows their mean. That spread is not pricing noise - the reconstruction scales with how many inference requests a run made (106, 118 and 128 across the three), so it tracks real differences in how much work the agent did. SEQUENTIAL legs throughout - one repo at a time, one machine, one key - which is what 'seq' means in the name and why this wall is comparable to the rest of the board. Effort verified active by a fresh zero-token ACP readback before each launch. Judge: Codex gpt-5.5 on all six legs.

## Qwen3.8-Flash (max effort)

**26 / 105** · repo 1 13/45 · repo 2 13/60 · 7 unplanted · 97.3 min · $1.81 · `max` · Claude Code / Alibaba API · single run · 2026-09-11

**Caveat.** One run at this tier, and repo 1 is a RETRY. The first repo-1 attempt at this exact setting made 70 successful tool calls across 73 turns with zero permission denials, then wrote 'Let me check a few remaining details before fixing...' and ended its turn having written no report - a healthy environment and a model that narrated its next step instead of taking it. The retry delivered 13/45 in 42.5 minutes, so that was a fluke rather than a property, but a top-effort run that silently stops on one repo in two attempts is itself a reliability signal, and the published wall and cost figures cover the retry only. Cost is a list-price ESTIMATE and not a bill - DashScope exposes no credits API on this path - computed from the run's own token counts at 0.15 in / 0.20 cache-write / 0.016 cache-read / 0.47 out per MTok. Do not read this row as a ceiling for the Qwen 3.8 family: it is the ceiling of the SMALL model. The Qwen3.8-Max row on this board ran a different path entirely (Claude Code -> local proxy -> OpenRouter) and is not a clean comparison.

The top of Alibaba's own accept-list on Alibaba's own Anthropic-compatible endpoint - no aggregator, no local proxy. 26/105 for $1.81 at list. Read it against its low companion, which is the point of running both: max buys THREE points over low (23/105) for 32% more money and the same wall clock (97.3 min against 98.1). The effort label is measured rather than requested - the endpoint 400-rejects an invented tier and names its accept-list (low, medium, high, xhigh, max), and an n=3-per-tier magnitude probe on this exact path shows the dial binds at the bottom: mean output tokens run low=3557, medium=4579, high=19329, and the 5.43x spread between lowest and highest mean clears the worst within-tier spread of 2.00x. So max is the real ceiling of a real dial. What it is NOT is a fifth rung: high (13933-27933), xhigh (12523-24778) and max (13767-19431) overlap almost entirely and their means FALL as the nominal tier rises, so paying for max over high buys nothing measurable. 26 strict matches, 3 partials, 3 claimed-only, 7 genuine unplanted extras.

This model's max rung replicated to three runs and landed below all of them, which is why the rungs beneath it are being measured at all: a sweep separates a weak model from a mis-set dial. *** ONE RUN IS ONE DRAW *** this board has measured a 13-point spread between two runs of a single identical max-effort configuration, and this model's own max rung spans three runs. Read any single rung, and any gap between two adjacent rungs, with that width in mind. *** CONFIGURED BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE MAX RUNG EXCEPT THE EFFORT STRING *** same model string gpt-6-sol, same pinned executable, same timeout, same price table, same blind judge. Flash's 23.3 is nominally higher at roughly half the cost per run ($0.49 against $0.86). Both means sit comfortably inside the other's run-to-run spread, so the claim this supports is PARITY, not victory: on this benchmark Xiaomi's cheap model is not measurably worse than its flagship. Read it that way rather than as a ranking. *** THE PARITY IS AN AVERAGE OF TWO OPPOSITE TILTS, NOT A UNIFORM MATCH *** Flash is weaker than Pro on repo 1 (8, 7, 9 of 45, against Pro's 9, 7 and 8) and stronger on repo 2 (11, 18, 17 of 60, against Pro's 11, 15 and 14). This route's `reasoning` field was measured inert on the Pro model (n=3 per condition, ranges fully overlapping); that probe was run on Pro, NOT on Flash, so for this model the dial is untested rather than known-inert. None of these three runs asserted a reasoning state. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL within every run, one repo at a time, and the three runs ran one at a time in order. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on all six legs - neither this model nor a sibling of it - with no judge switching anywhere in this mean. ## DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (max effort) - mean of 3

**21.7 / 105** · repo 1 11.3/45 · repo 2 10.3/60 · 6.7 unplanted · 55.9 min · $0.78 bill · `max` · Claude Code / DeepSeek API · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-15

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 19 to 24. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run max row (24/105) published 2026-09-10, which was the HIGHEST of the three; the supersession is about method, not a correction, and that receipt stands. Every figure here is a MEAN carried to one decimal - 21.7 of 105. The three runs were verified to have run in the same harness regime on both repos, which is the exception on this board rather than the rule; the CLI version differed (2.1.267 for the first run, 2.1.270 for the other two).

DeepSeek's top Flash rung, RUN THREE TIMES. Runs scored 24, 19 and 22 of 105 (repo 1: 14, 10, 10 - repo 2: 10, 9, 12). Mean 21.7, range 5. *** THE ONE ARM IN THIS WAVE WHOSE REPLICATION IS VERIFIED *** Three flash arms were replicated on the same nights; this is the only one where the harness left enough behind to check that the three runs ran in the same configuration. All six legs resolved deepseek-flash with the 1M window, and NONE of them compacted: peak live context 513,330 / 509,936 / 471,821 on repo 1 and 520,206 / 544,170 / 558,074 on repo 2, against a harness that would have thrown context away at about 30 per cent had it been triggered. A mean is a claim that its members differ only by chance, and on this row that claim was tested rather than assumed. *** ONE THING WAS NOT HELD FIXED *** The 2026-09-10 run ran Claude Code 2.1.267 and both replicates ran 2.1.270. The CLI self-updates between waves. It is stated rather than buried because the resolved model and the compaction behaviour are identical across all six legs, so the version string is the only difference the receipts show. *** WHAT THE SPREAD LOOKS LIKE *** The totals span 5 points, and the two repos moved in OPPOSITE directions: repo 1 fell 14, 10, 10 while repo 2 rose 10, 9, 12. That is an observation about three runs, not a mechanism, and it is not a claim that totals are steadier than legs - three draws cannot establish that. The published single run scored 24, the TOP of its own three-run range, so every comparison drawn against 24 was drawn against this configuration's best day. *** THE WALL COLUMN ON THIS ROW IS NOISY AND THE PER-LEG FIGURES ARE THE HONEST FORM *** Against the control's tokens per minute the four replicate legs ran 47, 131, 118 and 104 per cent - in both directions, same binary, same night. The 47 per cent leg is the one that overlapped hardest with two other arms running concurrently. Do not read a ratio off this row. *** EVER VS ALWAYS - THE THING A SINGLE RUN CANNOT SHOW *** Across the three runs this arm ever fixed 33 distinct bugs of 105, well above the 21.7 any single run manages. Of those 33, only 11 fell in ALL THREE runs; 10 fell in two and 12 in exactly one. A third of what it ever fixed is what it reliably fixes. It is the LEAST consistent of the three flash arms replicated in this wave despite scoring the highest of them, which is exactly the trade a single number hides. The Coverage view shades each tick by its observed hit rate, so this is readable on the row. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Per-leg (repo 1 / repo 2): wall 25.4/17.2, 59.9/18.1 and 20.6/26.4 minutes; bill 0.62/0.46, 0.30/0.29 and 0.31/0.36 dollars. Genuine extras 7, 6 and 7. Claimed-only 0, 2 and 1 across six legs, all on repo 2. Each leg is a full independent run of both repos; the mean is over three, and the per-repo runs are stated so the spread stays visible - it is the finding, not a caveat on it. billing=REAL bill: the runner snapshots the DeepSeek balance before and after each leg and writes the drop, so this cost column is money that moved, not a list estimate. Judge: Codex gpt-5.5 on all six legs.

## Fable 5.1 (medium effort)

**21 / 105** · repo 1 8/45 · repo 2 13/60 · 3 unplanted · 23.0 min · $23.73 · `medium` · Claude Code · single run · 2026-09-10

**Caveat.** One run per tier. A single non-monotonic point is a finding about this run, not a demonstrated property of the medium tier - the board's own variance evidence (three Grok 4.5 runs at one setting scoring 16, 13 and 17) is the reason that distinction is enforced here. What the row does establish is that the shell-first strategy and the low score arrived together, which is a mechanism worth having on record either way.

Fable 5.1's dial does not run in a straight line: medium scores 21/105, BELOW its own low row (29) and well below high (33) and max (43). The run is clean - both legs exited success, no truncation, no permission denials, no tool errors - and the raw captures say why in the tool mix rather than in the thinking. At medium the model worked almost entirely through the shell: 83 Bash calls against 3 Reads and ZERO Edits or Greps on repo 1, and 99 Bash against 2 Reads and zero Edits on repo 2. At low it read the codebase instead - 44 Reads, 20 Greps, 16 Edits on repo 1. That shows up in the billing shape too: medium pulled 4.0M cached-read tokens on repo 1 against low's 19.7M, because a session that never reads files into context keeps a small context to re-send. Fewer bugs found is what you would expect from a model that barely looked at the code.

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 16 to 25. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run xhigh row (20/105) published 2026-09-13, which landed within a point of the mean - the supersession is about method, not a correction. Every figure on this row is a MEAN of the three runs, carried to one decimal - 20.3 of 105. max has since been run three times too (33, 33, 35; mean 33.7, range 2), so nothing on this dial is n=1 any more. The FOURTH rung of Meta's first-party dial to be run three times, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 20, 25 and 16 of 105 (repo 1: 8, 10, 6 - repo 2: 12, 15, 10). Mean 20.3, range 9. *** WHERE THIS LEAVES THE DIAL *** ALL FIVE rungs have since been replicated: 9.7, 13.0, 18.7, 20.3 and 33.7. The dial is monotone across all five, and max was the last to be run three times. The step from high's mean to xhigh's is 1.7 points, against three-run ranges of 3 at high and 9 at xhigh, so these two rungs are not separated by anything this board can measure - which is what the single-run pair (19 and 20) already suggested and is now on firmer ground. *** A CLAIM THIS ROW DOES NOT MAKE, AND WHY IT IS WORTH SAYING *** The three-run ranges came out 9, 6, 3 and now 9. An earlier version of the low, medium and high rows read the first three as a spread that narrows as effort rises, and generalised it to every row on this board. That was withdrawn on 2026-09-14 BEFORE this rung was scored, because a range from three draws is a very noisy estimate of spread and three of them cannot establish a trend in variance at all. This rung landing at 9 is consistent with the withdrawal but is not the reason for it: a fourth number could not have settled a question that needs n in the tens per rung. The general rule this board now applies: a shape across a handful of summary statistics is an observation, and an observation is not a finding. *** WHAT IS SOLID HERE *** Two things, both counts rather than inferences. A fixed configuration - same model, same prompt, same harness, same route, same effort flag, same judge - produced totals of 16, 20 and 25 on this rung, a 9-point range on a 105-bug benchmark. And across its three runs this rung ever fixed 30 distinct bugs. Nothing about the distribution of repeats is claimed; those are counts of 8, 13 and 9 bugs and they move on very little. All six legs were clean: the Linux install returned 0, the shim logged zero errors, every leg exited 0, and no leg carries a handicap note. Per-leg receipts (repo 1 / repo 2): calls 417/206, 294/330, 242/325; wall 73.0, 61.7 and 56.5 minutes; cost 15.58, 13.17 and 12.38 dollars. ZERO claimed-only across all six legs. Genuine extras 7, 24 and 9. Each leg is a full independent run of both repos; the mean is over three, and the per-repo runs are stated so the spread stays visible - it is the finding, not a caveat on it. Difference -3.7. That 4.6 number falls OUTSIDE the spread of these three runs, which is the strongest form this comparison can take while the other side is still a single draw - a difference the variance seen here does not explain, not a measured gap between two distributions. *** WHERE THIS RUNG SITS ON ITS OWN DIAL *** The same model at xhigh is 28.8 of 105 over four runs; this rung is 19.3 over three, a gap of +9.5. Whether that flatness is a property of the dial or a coincidence of two single draws is exactly what replicating these rungs was for. *** WHAT THE SPREAD ITSELF SAYS *** 5 points between the best and worst run of an IDENTICAL configuration - the three arms are clones and differ only in name. Any single-run number at this rung, including the one this row replaces, was carrying that much slack without being able to show it. Read any n=1 row on this board, from any vendor, with that in mind. *** COST AND TIME *** Reconstructed floors of $11.54, $12.02, $13.32 for a mean of $12.29; walls 40.5, 33.6, 39.6 minutes, mean 37.9. COST IS A FLOOR, not a bill: the grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates on the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis, and xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates. This row uses the per-session arithmetic corrected on 2026-09-21; on the older run-level arithmetic that the 4.6 rows on this board still use it would read $12.29. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string AND its default is `high`, so a rung that failed to apply would look identical to one that worked in every artefact except that readback - the run would be a default-effort run wearing another label. The enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Sessions spawned per leg (repo 1 / repo 2): run 1 1 and 1; run 2 1 and 1; run 3 1 and 1. That count varies run to run on identical arm definitions for this model, tracks cost closely, and has tracked score not at all. The board's automatic regime check parses Claude Code transcripts, so it returns NOT CHECKED for every grok row here and the session counts above are the manual substitute. On the tier itself: Z.ai's endpoint 400-rejects an invented effort value and names its accept-list (none, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max), so unlike an aggregator that shrugs and returns 200, this route demonstrably READ the field - but a field being read is not the same as a dial being turned, and no magnitude probe was run here, so this row claims an accepted tier and not a measured one.

## Muse Spark 1.3 (high effort, Muse Code, Meta API) - mean of 3

**18.7 / 105** · repo 1 8.7/45 · repo 2 10/60 · 19.3 unplanted · 75.6 min · $12.94 · `high` · Muse Code / Meta API · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-14

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 17 to 20. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run high row (19/105) published 2026-09-13, which landed on the SAME number - the supersession is about method, not a correction, and the only claim it withdraws is that row's use of the high/xhigh pair as a spread estimate. Every figure on this row is a MEAN of the three runs, carried to one decimal - 18.7 of 105. Both rungs above this one have since been replicated as well: xhigh 20.3 (range 9) and max 33.7 (range 2). The 15-point step from high's mean to max's is measured at n=3 on both ends.

THIS ENDPOINT'S MEASURED DEFAULT RUNG, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 19, 17 and 20 of 105 (repo 1: 8, 10, 8 - repo 2: 11, 7, 12). *** THIS ONE CONFIRMS THE ROW IT WAS RUN TO CHECK *** The three legs average 18.7, which rounds to the 19 the single high run published on 2026-09-13. That is the opposite of what the same experiment did one and two rungs down, where the n=3 means reversed the published ordering of low and medium. It is worth saying plainly: replication is not a debunking tool, and this time the published number survived intact. *** WHAT THE THREE REPLICATED RUNGS SHOW, AND WHAT THEY DO NOT *** The observed ranges are low 12/13/4 (9), medium 9/15/15 (6), high 19/17/20 (3). That looks like a spread narrowing as the dial rises, and THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM IT. A range taken from three draws is a very noisy estimate of spread - three runs can fall 9, 6, 3 by chance alone - and three such estimates come nowhere near establishing a trend in variance, which would need far more runs per rung than this board is going to buy. Recorded as an OBSERVATION TO WATCH, not a finding. In particular, do NOT read it as 'a single run near a model's ceiling is safer than one at the bottom of its dial' - that is the same n=1 reasoning this experiment exists to reject, moved up a level, and no row on this board should be discounted or trusted on the strength of it. *** AND EFFORT BUYS RELIABILITY, NOT A LONGER TAIL OF LUCK *** Counting per BUG across each rung's three runs: low fixed 21 distinct bugs at least once and 2 of them every time, medium 23 ever and 4 always, high 30 ever and 8 always. The share fixed in ALL THREE runs climbs 10 to 17 to 27 per cent, while the fixed-exactly-once count barely moves (15, 11, 12). The direction that suggests is bugs PROMOTED from once to twice-or-always rather than a longer tail of one-off luck. SIZE OF THE EVIDENCE: the always column is 2, 4 and 8 bugs, counted over three runs apiece, so the percentages are illustrative and the direction is suggestive - neither is a measured reliability. The Coverage view shades each tick by the observed hit rate, so the raw counts are readable on the row itself rather than only the summary. *** THE COST OF A FIXED FLAG IS NOT FIXED *** Same arm, same tier, three runs: 10.81, 12.70 and 15.29 dollars, and 64.5, 85.8 and 76.7 minutes - a 41 per cent cost spread with nothing changed. Calls ran 373/226, 439/261 and 293/418 across the two repos. The monotone turns-track-score pattern visible in low's repo-1 legs does NOT reproduce here: repo 2's busiest leg scored BEST and repo 1's busiest scored two above its quietest. That supports the weaker claim already published at the bottom of this dial - the effort flag fixes a request field, not an amount of work, and the work performed varies by about a third inside one tier - and it does not support turns predicting score. *** WHAT THIS RETRACTS *** The single-run high row's caveat called high (19) and xhigh (20) sitting within a point of each other 'the closest thing to a run-to-run spread estimate this dial has'. With a real replicate the high spread is 3 points, so two rows one point apart were always inside one tier's noise and never carried that evidence. The CONCLUSION survives - high and xhigh remain indistinguishable - but it now rests on a measured spread instead of on two single runs agreeing by luck. All six legs were clean: the Linux install returned 0, the shim logged zero errors, every leg exited 0, and no leg carries a handicap note. Honesty profile across the three runs: 3 claimed-only in six legs (0, 2 and 1 by run) and 10, 20 and 28 genuine unplanted extras. No planted bug fell for the first time in either new leg, so the survivor count is unchanged. Each leg is a full independent run of both repos; the mean is over three, and the per-repo runs are stated so the spread stays visible - it is the finding, not a caveat on it. THE EFFORT LABEL DESCRIBES THE MAIN LOOP, NOT EVERY CALL THIS ROW IS BILLED FOR: Muse Code runs a reminder-observer side agent at fixed efforts the flag does not control. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: a subscription account with no per-run credits delta, costed at the same Meta list rates as the other first-party rows, which is what keeps them comparable. ctx 1M.

## MiMo-V2.6-Pro (first-party)

**18 / 105** · repo 1 9/45 · repo 2 9/60 · 4 unplanted · 74.9 min · $0.59 · `enabled` · Claude Code / Xiaomi first-party · single run · 2026-09-22

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean. Xiaomi's MiMo-V2.6-Pro driven by Claude Code through an Anthropic-API shim onto Xiaomi's OWN first-party endpoint - no aggregator hop at all, unlike the default row published earlier the same day. 18 of 105: 9 of 45 on repo 1 and 9 of 60 on repo 2, 4 genuine extras, 74.9 minutes, $0.59. ONE RUN. *** THIS MODEL HAS NO GRADUATED EFFORT DIAL, ON ANY ROUTE - CONFIRMED TWO WAYS *** Xiaomi's own docs document exactly one reasoning control for this model: `thinking.type` = enabled or disabled, a binary toggle, not a low/medium/high/xhigh scale. Confirmed directly, not just from docs: a 2-call test against this same endpoint found thinking=disabled gives 0 reasoning tokens (542 completion tokens total) and thinking=enabled gives 2,375 chars of reasoning content (1,696 completion tokens). This also explains the earlier OpenRouter finding on the default row - reasoning.effort was accepted but inert there (n=3 per condition, low/high/no-field ranges fully overlapping) because OpenRouter was normalising onto this SAME binary toggle, and no effort value it sent could ever separate on a route with only two states. *** THIS ROW ASSERTS THE TOGGLE EXPLICITLY *** Every call carries thinking={type:enabled}, forced by the shim regardless of what the harness sent, verbatim Claude-shaped (the field name and structure this endpoint documents). Labelled `enabled`, not `max` or `high`: borrowing a graduated-tier name would imply a scale this model does not have. *** COST IS A LIST-PRICE ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL *** $0.59 is token counts against Xiaomi's own published per-token rate (0.435 in / 0.0036 cache-read / 0.87 out per MTok) - real usage at a real rate, but this platform's balance/usage API was not checked the way OpenRouter's credits-delta was for the other row, so it is not called a bill. *** RECEIPTS *** Key is a budget-capped ($5) first-party test key. Both legs exited 0, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. The run shared the laptop with benchmark legs from other vendors, which the wall marker on this row discloses - scores, tokens and cost are untouched by that, only the clock. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it. Second measurement of this model, published as its own row rather than averaged into the first: route and an explicitly-asserted effort are configuration, not noise.

## Hy4 Preview

**18 / 105** · repo 1 9/45 · repo 2 9/60 · 2 unplanted · 67.5 min · $3.13 bill · `default` · Claude Code / OpenRouter · single run · 2026-08-28

Tencent Hunyuan 4 preview (MoE, 49B active of 770B), benchmarked day-one via OpenRouter. 18/105 for $3.13 real OpenRouter bill. On repo 1 it claimed 10 fixes and 9 verified against the answer key. Single host (Tencent Cloud); day-one 429/404s absorbed by proxy retries. EFFORT CORRECTED 2026-09-12, from `high / first_party` to `default / inert_default`. The day-one row took Tencent's documentation at its word - reasoning_effort is a BINARY switch, `high` (the default) or `no_think`, so there is no higher tier to ask for - and published the tier the arm REQUESTED. Probing the route it actually ran on says the request never arrived: at n=3 per condition on OpenRouter's /v1/messages, both spellings overlap the no-field baseline, the baseline is the HIGHEST band of the five (mean 13,520 output tokens against 8,243 for nested high), and `bogus_zzz` returns HTTP 200 on both spellings while an invented model id 400s - no validation, so nothing downstream reads the field. Receipt: results/effort-dial-probes/20260912-hy4preview-openrouter.txt. The row is still at the model's ceiling, but for a different reason than it claimed: not because the tier was asserted and applied, but because Tencent's documented default IS the top of a two-position switch. That is a fact about Tencent's serving, not about this run - which is the whole distinction the correction makes.

## Gemini 3.8 Flash - mean of 3

**18 / 105** · repo 1 6.7/45 · repo 2 11.3/60 · 4 unplanted · 38.7 min · $11.03 · `high` · Antigravity CLI · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-15

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 15 to 20. This row SUPERSEDES the single-run row (20/105) published 2026-09-02, which was the HIGHEST of the three; the supersession is about method, not a correction. Every figure here is a MEAN carried to one decimal - 18.0 of 105. THE REPLICATION IS NOT VERIFIED: the Antigravity CLI writes no init record, so neither the context regime nor the harness version is recoverable for any of the three runs. AND THE WALL COLUMN IS NOT COMPARABLE TO ITS CONTROL: all four replicate legs ran 17 to 32 per cent slower per token than the 2026-09-02 run, and the cause is not established - box load and provider throttling were both tested and both failed to explain it.

Google's newest Flash on its own agent CLI, RUN THREE TIMES. Runs scored 20, 19 and 15 of 105 (repo 1: 7, 7, 6 - repo 2: 13, 12, 9). Mean 18.0, range 5. *** THE REPLICATION IS UNVERIFIED, AND THIS HARNESS IS THE BLINDEST ON THE BOARD *** The Antigravity CLI writes no session init record at all, so nothing about context size, compaction or even its own version is recoverable from the transcripts. Where the Z.ai rows are missing token counts, this one is missing the record that would carry them. So it is not known whether the three runs met the same conditions, and it is not known what harness version the 2026-09-02 control ran against the 2026-09-15 replicates. Stated plainly rather than left to be inferred from an absence. *** THE WALL COLUMN IS SLOWER THAN ITS CONTROL AND NOBODY KNOWS WHY *** All four replicate legs ran at 68, 71, 74 and 83 per cent of the 2026-09-02 control's tokens per minute - 17 to 32 per cent slower, every leg, in one direction. TWO EXPLANATIONS WERE CHECKED AND BOTH FAILED. Box contention: the GLM arm ran under the identical three-lane load at 100 and 143 per cent of its own control, so a busy machine does not explain it. Provider throttling: one leg did die on a hard Google quota wall (Individual quota reached, resets in 3h34m11s) and was re-run, and the throttling story predicted that the re-run - alone on an idle box with a freshly reset quota, the cleanest conditions any leg in this wave got - would come back near the control's rate. It came back the SLOWEST OF THE FOUR. A hypothesis that predicts fast and gets the slowest run of the set under its own best conditions is refuted, not refined. THE CAUSE IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and no cause is asserted on this row. What it does not touch: the scores. Every score on this board is judged from the diff against a withheld answer key, so wall and cost are the only columns a slow leg can move. *** WHAT THE SCORES SHOW *** The published single run scored 20, the TOP of the three. Repo 2 carries almost all of this arm's score and all of its movement (13, 12, 9 against repo 1's 7, 7, 6), and repo 1 produced ZERO genuine unplanted extras on all three runs. *** EVER VS ALWAYS *** Across the three runs this arm ever fixed 24 distinct bugs of 105 against a mean of 18.0, and 14 of those 24 fell in ALL THREE runs - 2 in two, 8 in exactly one. It fixes the FEWEST distinct bugs of the three flash arms replicated in this wave and repeats the largest share of them. ## GLM-5.3 Flash (high effort, Z.ai API)

**16 / 105** · repo 1 9/45 · repo 2 7/60 · 4 unplanted · 62.3 min · $0.94 · `high` · Claude Code / Z.ai API · single run · 2026-09-13

**Caveat.** One run at each of the three tiers, so every step on this dial is n=1 against n=1. The monotonic ordering across three rungs is more than any single pair would give, but it is three single runs and a repeat at any rung could reorder the middle. THE 3-POINT GAP TO max IS NOT CLAIMED AS REAL. It is smaller than the 6-point OpenRouter gap this board declined to credit on the max row, and it should be read the same way: high and max are close enough that this pair does not separate them. What the dial DOES separate is low from the other two, by 7 and 10 points. WALL CLOCK ON THIS ROW IS NOT A CLEAN-ROOM FIGURE AND THE AUTOMATIC MARKER DID NOT CATCH IT. The repo 2 leg overlapped a Meta arm on a different provider for about 3 of its 32 minutes, before that arm was stopped. *** THIS SENTENCE GAVE THE WRONG REASON AND IS CORRECTED 2026-09-13, SAME DAY *** The runner does NOT only snapshot at the start: it samples the live-leg registry at leg start AND at leg end and unions the two, and has since cross-provider parallelism was first allowed. A leg joined later IS caught if it is still running at the end. What actually happened here is narrower: the Meta arm both ARRIVED and DEPARTED between those two samples - it joined about 3 minutes in and was force-killed before this leg finished - so it existed at neither endpoint. Two point samples cannot see an interval that fits strictly between them. Superseded wording, kept verbatim rather than deleted: the runner stamps concurrent_with= by snapshotting the live-leg registry when a leg STARTS, so a leg that is JOINED later records nothing - this one started first and carries no marker. Three minutes of a mostly-installing neighbour is a negligible effect on a 62-minute total and it can only inflate, never shrink, but the row says it rather than implying a measurement it did not make. CLOSED 2026-09-13, after the wave: the runner now samples the peer registry on every tick of the 30-second keep-alive sweep it already runs, and the row carries the union across all samples - so a peer that lives longer than one tick can no longer slip between the endpoints. Verified against a transient peer that is invisible to both. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: Z.ai publishes no balance or usage endpoint on this plan key, so no credits delta exists to read; costed at the same published Flash rates the other three Flash rows were costed at, which keeps them comparable - and those rates carry a launch discount that can lapse. This endpoint does not report reasoning tokens separately, so output volume is the only work proxy available across the dial. ctx 1M.

The MIDDLE rung of Z.ai's three-tier dial, and the row that completes it. low, high and max are the entire accept-list for this family - none, minimal, medium, xhigh and an invented value are all refused HTTP 400 code 1210 - so EVERY TIER THIS ENDPOINT WILL SERVE FOR THIS MODEL NOW HAS A BENCH ROW, which no other model on this board can say. *** THE DIAL IS MONOTONIC ON SCORE *** 9 at low, 16 here, 19 at max, with output volume rising alongside it - 36,777 then 97,332 then 115,653 tokens. This is a dial that works, measured end to end on one serving path with the route, the harness, the model, the prompt and the repos all held fixed. *** AND IT IS NOT MONOTONIC ON VALUE, WHICH IS THE MORE USEFUL HALF *** Cost per strict fix runs 4.6 cents at low, 5.9 cents HERE, and 5.0 cents at max. THE MIDDLE RUNG IS THE WORST BUY ON THE DIAL. Muse Spark 1.3 did the same thing hours earlier on Meta's stack, where xhigh cost 78 cents per fix against 57 at high and 60 at max. Two vendors, two unrelated dials, two different harnesses, same shape - the middle is where the money goes and the score does not follow. That is not something a tier name or a token sweep would tell you. *** ON THIS STACK high IS DOMINATED BY max, ON ALL THREE COLUMNS AT ONCE *** high costs 0.94 dollars against max's 0.95, takes LONGER in wall clock - 62.3 minutes against 58.3 - and scores three fewer. There is no reading of those numbers on which a buyer should pick high here, which is worth saying plainly because the tier name suggests otherwise. *** IT ALSO LOCATES THE OPENROUTER FLASH ROW, WITHOUT SETTLING IT *** That row scores 13 with 70,628 output tokens. Both figures sit BETWEEN low and high on this dial, not at the top of it - so whatever OpenRouter serves for this model, it is demonstrably not the ceiling, even though Z.ai documents max as this family's default. What tier it IS serving remains unknown: that route ignores the effort field this board sends, which is what inert_default records, and nothing here identifies what it substitutes. Honesty profile: 9 of 45 on repo 1 and 7 of 60 on repo 2, no partials on either, ONE claimed-only per repo, and 4 genuine unplanted extras. Two claimed-only sits between the max row's one and the OpenRouter row's five, so the claim record does not track the tier.

## Grok 4.7 (low) - mean of 3

**15.7 / 105** · repo 1 10.3/45 · repo 2 5.3/60 · 18.3 unplanted · 22.2 min · $9.36 floor · `low` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-21

**Caveat.** Three runs of an identical configuration spanning 11 to 21 of 105. The Grok 4.6 row at this rung is a SINGLE run, so that comparison is three draws against one and is not a comparison of two distributions. Cost is a reconstructed FLOOR rather than a bill, and uses a per-session arithmetic the 4.6 rows on this board do not yet use; the note gives both figures. The runs spawned different numbers of subagent sessions on identical arm definitions, which the board's regime check cannot verify on this harness.

Grok 4.7 at low through xAI's own CLI, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 11, 21 and 15 of 105 (repo 1: 5, 15, 11 of 45 - repo 2: 6, 6, 4 of 60). Mean 15.7, spread 10. This supersedes the single-run row published earlier the same day at 11 of 105, which said the same thing on one draw. *** AGAINST THE PREVIOUS GENERATION AT THE SAME RUNG *** Grok 4.6 at low scored 15 of 105 (6 on repo 1, 9 on repo 2) - A SINGLE RUN, so the samples are not the same size and the comparison is three draws against one. Difference +0.7. That 4.6 number falls INSIDE the spread of these three runs, which is the clearest way to say the two are not distinguishable on this evidence. *** WHERE THIS RUNG SITS ON ITS OWN DIAL *** The same model at xhigh is 28.8 of 105 over four runs; this rung is 15.7 over three, a gap of +13.1. The dial as the board now shows it: low 15.7 (n=3), medium 26.7 (n=3), high 19.3 (n=3), xhigh 28.8 (n=4). For comparison Grok 4.6's dial ran low 15, medium 23, high 23, xhigh 28.7 - FLAT between medium and high, each of those from a single run. Whether that flatness is a property of the dial or a coincidence of two single draws is exactly what replicating these rungs was for. *** WHAT THE SPREAD ITSELF SAYS *** 10 points between the best and worst run of an IDENTICAL configuration - the three arms are clones and differ only in name. Any single-run number at this rung, including the one this row replaces, was carrying that much slack without being able to show it. Read any n=1 row on this board, from any vendor, with that in mind. *** COST AND TIME *** Reconstructed floors of $5.41, $10.88, $11.78 for a mean of $9.36; walls 15.1, 20.1, 31.3 minutes, mean 22.2. COST IS A FLOOR, not a bill: the grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates on the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis, and xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates. This row uses the per-session arithmetic corrected on 2026-09-21; on the older run-level arithmetic that the 4.6 rows on this board still use it would read $9.71. Compare cost across rows only where both use the same arithmetic. *** GENUINE EXTRAS *** Real defects found in the repo that are not part of the planted set ran 12, 25 and 18 across the three runs, a mean of 18.3. Extras are not scored; they are reported because a model that finds real bugs nobody planted is doing something the planted-bug total cannot see. *** THE TIER WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED, AND AT THE LOWER RUNGS THAT MATTERS MOST *** A zero-token ACP readback before each of these three launches returned reasoning_effort low for a requested low, with the served model named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string AND its default is `high`, so a rung that failed to apply would look identical to one that worked in every artefact except that readback - the run would be a default-effort run wearing another label. The enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL within every run, one repo at a time, and only one grok run on the machine at a time - two grok agents share one login. Sessions spawned per leg (repo 1 / repo 2): run 1 1 and 1; run 2 4 and 2; run 3 3 and 1. That count varies run to run on identical arm definitions for this model, tracks cost closely, and has tracked score not at all. The board's automatic regime check parses Claude Code transcripts, so it returns NOT CHECKED for every grok row here and the session counts above are the manual substitute. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on all six legs, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Qwen3.8-27B (xhigh effort, Alibaba API) - mean of 3

**15 / 105** · repo 1 5.7/45 · repo 2 9.3/60 · 2.3 unplanted · 56.8 min · $5.55 · `xhigh` · Claude Code / Alibaba API · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-15

**Caveat.** Every figure on this row is a MEAN of three runs, carried to one decimal - 15.0 of 105, range 4 (13 to 17). This row SUPERSEDES the single-run row (15/105) published 2026-09-12, which landed exactly on this mean by coincidence of one draw, not because a single run and a mean agree in general - see the range before reading any margin drawn against 15 as precise. effort_status stays first_party rather than verified for the same reason it did at n=1: what is verified is that the field is accepted and validated, not that xhigh buys more thinking than a lower tier on this specific model - the magnitude sweep exists for the other two Qwen3.8 sizes and not for this one. Cost is a list-price ESTIMATE, not a bill, as above. The three runs were verified to have run in the same harness regime on both repos - same model resolved, zero compactions across all six legs, peak live context 26,047-27,567 against a 1,000,000-token declared window - which is the exception on this board rather than the rule. One thing was not held fixed and is on the record: CLI 2.1.268 for the first run, 2.1.272 for the other two.

THE MIDDLE SIZE OF THE QWEN3.8 FAMILY, RUN THREE TIMES ON ALIBABA'S OWN FIRST-PARTY ENDPOINT. Runs scored 15, 13 and 17 of 105 (repo 1: 6, 5, 6 - repo 2: 9, 8, 11). Mean 15.0, range 4. *** THE FINDING FROM THE SINGLE RUN SURVIVES AT n=3 *** The n=1 row (15/105) was the most interesting Qwen row on the board because it LOST to both siblings and cost more than one of them. The mean does not soften that: at 15.0 it is still eleven points below Qwen3.8-Flash's 26/105 and well below Qwen3.8-Max's 28/105, on the same endpoint, the same harness and the same two repos. It is still the fast one - a combined wall mean of 56.8 minutes against Flash's 97.3 and Max's 103.4 - so read it as a latency option rather than a price/capability midpoint; on capability per dollar there is still no case for it here. *** THE EFFORT LABEL IS MEASURED, NOT REQUESTED, AND THAT DOES NOT CHANGE WITH REPLICATION *** This arm is commissioned at DEFAULT effort. On this route there is no such thing: a logging pass-through of the CLI (receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260912-claude-code-wire-default-effort.txt) shows an unflagged Claude Code putting output_config.effort=xhigh on every main call, and the endpoint validates and echoes that field. The dial itself was probed once, not three times - it is a property of the endpoint's wire protocol, not of an individual run, so it does not need re-verifying per leg. Honesty profile across the three runs, mean per repo: repo 1 fixed 5.7/45 with zero partials, repo 2 fixed 9.3/60 with 0.7 partials; claimed-only 2.0 and 2.3; genuine unplanted extras 0.7 and 1.7; false-positive fixes zero on both, all three runs. Per-leg receipts (repo 1 / repo 2): wall 24.8/22.0, 24.5/43.4 and 26.8/28.8 minutes; cost 2.48/3.61, 1.63/3.60 and 2.11/3.23 dollars. All six legs were clean - every leg exited 0, none carries a handicap note. ONE WALL FIGURE IS FLAGGED, NOT EXPLAINED: two of the three repo-2 legs shared the machine with another running leg, and this board's own metrics record that as contention rather than silently folding it into the mean. Contention can inflate a wall figure, never shrink it, and it does not touch score, tokens or cost. The two contended legs landed nowhere near each other (43.4 and 28.8 minutes) and the one uncontended control (22.0) is the fastest of the three, so the direction is consistent with contention costing time but the SIZE of the effect is not - no cause beyond 'shared the box' is asserted here. Each leg is a full independent run of both repos; the mean is over three, and the per-repo runs are stated so the spread stays visible. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: DashScope exposes no credits API on this path, so cost is computed from each run's own token counts at 0.425 in / 0.5312 cache-write / 0.085 cache-read / 2.55 out per MTok - the same rates the n=1 row used, from OpenRouter's Alibaba-endpoint listing for this slug. Cache writes are priced and not zero: Qwen is the one family on this board that is charged for them.
