## Grok 4.7 (medium)

**30 / 105** · repo 1 11/45 · repo 2 19/60 · 30 unplanted · 32.4 min · $25.15 floor · `medium` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · single run · 2026-09-21

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-21 by the n=3 row at the same configuration (26.7 of 105). The three runs scored 30, 23 and 27, so this single draw at 30 sat at the edge of a 7-point spread it could not show. Nothing it claimed is retracted; it was published as an n=1 row and read as one. Kept rather than deleted because it is the receipt for what the board showed for part of 2026-09-21.

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean. The same model at its top rung spanned 25 to 31 of 105 across three runs, so read this number with several points of slack in either direction. Cost is a reconstructed FLOOR rather than a bill, and it uses a per-session arithmetic the older grok rows on this board do not yet use; the note gives both figures.

Grok 4.7 at medium through xAI's own CLI - the MEDIUM rung, one step below the CLI default. 30 of 105: 11 of 45 on repo 1 and 19 of 60 on repo 2, 30 genuine extras, 32.4 minutes, a reconstructed floor of $25.15. ONE RUN. *** AGAINST THE PREVIOUS GENERATION AT THE SAME RUNG *** Grok 4.6 (medium) scored 23.0 of 105 (7 on repo 1, 16 on repo 2), also a single run, for a difference of +7. Both sides are n=1, so this is one draw against one draw. The scale of the doubt is not a guess: three runs of the 4.6 configuration one rung up spanned 27 to 30 of 105, and three runs of this model at that rung spanned 25 to 31 - so a gap of a few points between two single runs is inside what the same configuration produces run to run. *** WHERE IT SITS ON ITS OWN DIAL *** This model at xhigh is 28.8 of 105, published as a mean of four. This rung is 30, a single run. The 4.6 dial for comparison ran low 15, medium 23, high 23, xhigh 28.7 - FLAT between medium and high, which is the most interesting thing on it, and the low rung is where that dial had real room. Whether either shape repeats here is a question the rungs answer together: read this row beside its siblings rather than on its own. *** THE TIER WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED, AND FOR THE LOWER RUNGS THAT MATTERS MORE *** A zero-token ACP readback before launch returned reasoning_effort medium for a requested medium. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string AND its default is `high`, so a rung that failed to apply would look identical to one that worked in every artefact except this readback - the run would simply be a default-effort run wearing another label. The enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh. The served model is named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. *** COST IS A FLOOR *** The grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates, the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis. This row uses the per-session arithmetic corrected on 2026-09-21; on the older run-level arithmetic the other grok rows still use it would read $28.86. It is a floor twice over: xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates. *** RECEIPTS *** Both legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL, one repo at a time, uncontended - nothing else ran alongside. The agent spawned 3 session(s) on repo 1 and 5 on repo 2 (133 and 199 inference requests); that count varies run to run on identical arm definitions for this model, and it tracks cost far more closely than score. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Grok 4.7 (xhigh) seq - mean of 3

**28.7 / 105** · repo 1 13.3/45 · repo 2 15.3/60 · 31.7 unplanted · 46.2 min · $23.17 floor · `xhigh` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · mean of 3 runs · 2026-09-21

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-21 by the n=4 row at the same configuration (28.8). A fourth run scored 29 of 105 and moved the mean by +0.1 of a bug, so nothing this row claimed is retracted - it said the generation step moved nothing that is being counted, and a fourth draw said the same on more evidence. What the newer row can say that this one could not: the wall comparison, which this row published as undecidable because only one of its three runs was uncontended. The fourth run was also uncontended, and the two clean runs put this model within about a minute and a half of Grok 4.6. Kept rather than deleted because it is the receipt for what the board showed for part of 2026-09-21.

**Caveat.** A mean of three runs, not a single measurement; the three span 25 to 31 of 105 against 4.6's 27 to 30, so this tie rests on the wider of the two distributions. Cost uses a per-session reconstruction the other grok rows do not yet use; the note gives both figures and the gap holds either way. The wall is contended upward on four of the six legs and is not a basis for a speed comparison. The three runs spawned different numbers of subagent sessions on identical arm definitions, which the board's regime check cannot verify on this harness.

Grok 4.7 at xhigh through xAI's own CLI, the first 4.7 row on this board, published as the MEAN OF THREE INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 25, 30 and 31 of 105 (repo 1: 12, 12, 16 - repo 2: 13, 18, 15). Mean 28.7, range 6. *** THE HEADLINE IS THAT NOTHING MOVED *** Grok 4.6 at the SAME tier, also a mean of three, stands at 28.7 of 105. This row is 28.7. Three runs a side at a verified ceiling, and the totals are identical to the decimal - the cleanest null result this board has produced for a generation step. It is published as one. A tie is a measurement, and publishing only when a number improves would make this a scoreboard for vendors rather than a benchmark. *** THE IDENTICAL TOTAL HIDES A SWAP *** Repo 1 scored 12, 12, 16 of 45 against 4.6's mean of 11.7; repo 2 scored 13, 18, 15 of 60 against 17.0. Up about a point and a half on one codebase, down about the same on the other. Both movements sit inside the spread of the runs that produced them, so this is a direction worth testing and not a finding. *** WHAT DOES MOVE, AND IT MOVES THE WRONG WAY: COST *** Reconstructed floors of $16.54, $23.04, $29.94 for a mean of $23.17, against $16.89 for the three 4.6 runs on the SAME arithmetic - about 37% more for the same score. READ THE COST COLUMN CAREFULLY: the reconstruction used to take the largest context in a run and charge every request in that run against it, which overstates any run that fans out into subagents by up to 25%, and it now measures each session separately. This row carries the corrected figure. The 4.6 row still shows $18.60 on the old arithmetic and has not yet been restated; on that same old arithmetic these three runs come to $26.18. So the gap is about a third either way. What is inconsistent across the board right now is the arithmetic, not the finding. Both numbers are floors twice over: the grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates - the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis, and xAI prices 4.7 identically to 4.6 - and xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates, which these runs did, by an amount this method cannot see. *** AND WHAT MOVES IN 4.7'S FAVOUR: BUGS NOBODY PLANTED *** Genuine extras - real defects found in the repo that are not part of the planted set - ran 26, 35, 34 across the three runs, a mean of 31.7, against 18.0 for 4.6: roughly 76% more. The most defensible reading of this wave is that 4.7 finds materially more real bugs than 4.6 and no more of the ones being counted. Whether that is a better bug-hunter or a worse instruction-follower is a judgment the number does not settle. *** PER BUG, THE TWO MODELS ARE NEARLY THE SAME MODEL *** Across three runs a side, on repo 1 each touched 18 distinct planted bugs with 15 shared; on repo 2 it was 21 against 22 with 18 shared. There is not one bug on either repo that 4.6 found on all three runs and 4.7 never found - zero hard regressions - and exactly one that 4.7 found on all three and 4.6 never did. Every other bug unique to one model was a single-run hit: noise, not capability. Consistency shifted in opposite directions per repo: 4.7 holds 10 bugs on all three runs against 4.6's 5 on repo 1, and 10 against 14 on repo 2. *** THE RUNS DID NOT BEHAVE ALIKE, AND THE AUTOMATIC CHECK CANNOT SEE IT HERE *** The regime check that refuses a mean over mismatched configurations parses Claude Code transcripts, so it returns NOT CHECKED for every grok row on this board. The CLI's own session logs show what it would have looked for. Sessions spawned per leg (repo 1 / repo 2) - this model: run 1 1 and 1; run 2 1 and 3; run 3 5 and 1. The 4.6 rows at the same tier: run 1 3 and 3; run 2 4 and 3; run 3 5 and 3. 4.6 never once ran a leg as a single session; this model did it four times out of six, and when it does fan out it commits far harder - 184 and 206 inference requests against 4.6's 88 to 128. Fan-out tracks COST almost perfectly across these runs and tracks SCORE not at all: 4.6's weakest repo-1 leg fanned out to three sessions, and the two single-session repo-1 legs here scored the same as each other. The arm definitions are byte-identical clones, so this is emergent agent behaviour rather than configuration - the same class of hidden difference the regime check was built for. An OBSERVATION over three runs, not a mechanism. *** THE TIER IS THE MODEL CEILING AND THAT WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED *** A zero-token ACP readback before each launch returned reasoning_effort xhigh for a requested xhigh, and the enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh with high as the CLI default - nothing above xhigh exists to ask for. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string, so an echo is the only thing that makes this label honest. The served model is named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. *** RECEIPTS *** All six legs exited 0. Per-run wall 39.6, 47.3, 51.8 minutes against 43.1 for the 4.6 mean - but that comparison is NOT decidable from this wave: only run 1 had the machine to itself, runs 2 and 3 overlapped each other, and contention biases a wall upward. On the one uncontended run this was the fastest run at this tier the board has recorded. Legs SEQUENTIAL within every run, one repo at a time - that is what seq means here. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on all six legs, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Qwen3.8-Max (max effort, Alibaba API)

**28 / 105** · repo 1 13/45 · repo 2 15/60 · 6 unplanted · 103.4 min · $26.29 · `max` · Claude Code / Alibaba API · single run · 2026-09-11

**Superseded.** Qwen3.8-Max (max effort, Alibaba API) - mean of 3

**Caveat.** One run at this tier. The effort ceiling is real but flat: the same probe shows high (9946-14061), xhigh (8266-19309) and max (6542-14902) are mutually indistinguishable with means that fall as the nominal tier rises, so max is the top of the dial and buys nothing measurable over high - the same two-rung shape the Qwen3.8-Flash probe found, which suggests it is a property of Alibaba's serving rather than of either model. Note also that this endpoint's accept-list CONTRADICTS the QwenCloud doc the superseded row relied on (low|medium|xhigh with high->xhigh), which is one more reason that row's tier claim was never safe. Cost is a list-price ESTIMATE and not a bill - DashScope exposes no credits API on this path - computed from the run's own token counts at 2.00 in / 2.50 cache-write / 0.25 cache-read / 6.00 out per MTok, from OpenRouter's Alibaba-endpoint listing. Finally, do NOT read the 9-point gain as caused by the path. It is n=1 against n=1 and almost all of it is one repo - repo 1 went 5/45 to 13/45 while repo 2 moved by one - and this board has seen the same model at the same setting swing 6 points on repo 1 (Opus 5 at medium: 11/8/14). A systematic path effect should have moved both repos and did not, so the score difference is unexplained and may simply be variance. What the pair DOES establish beyond variance is the cost and token behaviour, where the gap is ~5,000x and not a coin flip.

The Aug 3 Qwen3.8-Max row re-run on Alibaba's OWN Anthropic-compatible endpoint - no local proxy, no aggregator - and it is the clearest demonstration on this board that a row records a SERVING PATH and not just a model. Same model, same harness, same two repos, same laptop, one hop removed: 28/105 against 19/105, 103.4 min against 148.1, $26.29 against $33.30. Nine points more for a fifth less money and a third less time, and the gain is concentrated in repo 1, which went 5/45 to 13/45. The mechanism is visible in the token columns. The OpenRouter run billed 12.94% of its prompt tokens as UNCACHED INPUT - 8,282,777 of them, $16.57, half that row's cost - while this one billed 0.0019%, or 1,716 tokens across both legs. Caching that keeps working means the model keeps its context instead of rebuilding it, which is why output ROSE 23% (168,395 tokens against 136,426) while wall time FELL: it did more thinking and less re-reading. Effort here is measured, not requested - the endpoint 400-rejects an invented tier and names its accept-list (low, medium, high, xhigh, max), and an n=3-per-tier probe on this exact path separates the bottom of the dial cleanly (mean output tokens low=4495 against high=12422, low's range overlapping none of the top three, 3.09x between-tier ratio against a 2.34x worst within-tier spread). 28 strict matches, 2 partials, 3 claimed-only, 6 genuine unplanted extras.

## DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (max effort)

**24 / 105** · repo 1 14/45 · repo 2 10/60 · 7 unplanted · 42.6 min · $1.08 bill · `max` · Claude Code / DeepSeek API · single run · 2026-09-10

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-15 by the n=3 MEAN row at the same rung (21.7/105). This row is ONE run of three and it is the HIGHEST of the three: identical runs scored 24, 19 and 22. Nothing in it is retracted - the 24 happened and this is its receipt - but it was the configuration's best day, so any comparison drawn against 24 was drawn against that. Kept rather than deleted because a supersession is only legible next to the row it supersedes.

**Caveat.** The endpoint deserializer also accepts an undocumented `ultra`, which DeepSeek own docs never mention (they give none|low|high|max, default high). Sampled n=4 it straddles both high and max and resolves to neither, and nothing in that sample exceeds max band - so `max` is the top of the documented dial and nothing measured reasons more than it, which is what the ceiling label claims. Which tier `ultra` aliases is unresolved and left that way rather than guessed.

DeepSeek newest Flash generation, benchmarked on DeepSeek OWN Anthropic-compatible API - no aggregator, no proxy. Claude Code puts --effort on the wire as output_config.effort; the endpoint 400-rejects an invented tier and names its enum, and a thinking-volume sweep separates the tiers with DISJOINT ranges (high mean 30.5K chars, max mean 83.6K, n=3), so max was applied and not merely requested. Cost is a real bill, not an estimate: DeepSeek sells prepaid credit, so each leg is charged at the balance drop across it. $1.08 of real credit for 24/105 - the same score as Fable 5 and as Opus 5 at medium. Those two are list estimates and this is a bill, so read the gap as an order of magnitude and not as a ratio.

**24 / 105** · repo 1 12/45 · repo 2 12/60 · 9.5 unplanted · 11.3 min · $8.73 · `low` · Claude Code · mean of 2 runs · 2026-09-23

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-23 by the mean of 3 (22.3 of 105). This row reported 24.0 of 105 as the mean of 2 runs. The replicates are byte-identical re-runs; across 3 the runs span 19 to 25. Nothing here is retracted - it is kept as the receipt for what the board showed before the extra run.

Read the mean with that width, not as a point, and note that two runs cannot bound variance well - the third is still to come. Anthropic's Opus 5.5 at low effort through Claude Code on the first-party API, published as the MEAN OF TWO INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 23 and 25 of 105 (repo 1: 11, 13 of 45 - repo 2: 12, 12 of 60). Mean 24.0, spread 2. *** THIS SUPERSEDES OPUS 5.5 (LOW EFFORT) (23 OF 105) *** *** WHY THE REPLICATES EXIST *** the first pass measured this ladder at one run per rung and it came out monotone - medium 30, high 32, xhigh 35, max 43. That was suggestive and nothing more: no gap in it exceeded the 13-point spread this board has measured between two runs of ONE identical max-effort configuration, so the ordering of any two adjacent rungs was unresolved and every note said so. Three runs per rung is what can resolve it, and the whole ladder is being re-measured that way rather than only its top. The board currently reads low 22.3 (n=3), medium 30.3 (n=3), high 31.7 (n=3), xhigh 36 (n=3), max 41.7 (n=3). *** THE REPLICATES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE FIRST RUN *** same model string claude-opus-5-5[1m], same effort, same timeout, same price table, same blind judge. Only the arm name differs, so the spread above is run-to-run variance and nothing else. *** WALL AND COST *** Walls 12.4, 10.2 minutes, mean 11.3; cost $8.68, $8.79, mean $8.73. COST IS A LIST ESTIMATE at Anthropic's published Opus 5.5 rates - $4 input, $20 output, $0.20 cache read (a 0.05x cache multiplier unique to this model, where every other model here uses 0.1x) and $8 cache write, the 1-hour rate, which is what this harness actually writes and what the streams confirm. Not an invoice, hence `list`. *** GENUINE EXTRAS *** 14 and 5 across the runs, mean 9.5 - real defects found in the repos that nobody planted, reported separately and never folded into the score. *** THE CONTEXT WINDOW *** every run here holds a 1M window and crosses zero compaction boundaries, so these rows compare cleanly to each other and NOT to the Opus 5 ladder, which ran the CLI's default 200K and compacted repeatedly mid-task. Autocompact ran at the CLI's own default throughout. *** RECEIPTS *** All legs exited 0, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5 on every leg - an OpenAI model scoring an Anthropic one, so neither the model itself nor a sibling of it. The runs shared the laptop with legs from other vendors, which the wall marker discloses; scores, tokens and cost are untouched by that, only the clock.

## MiMo-V2.6-Pro

**20 / 105** · repo 1 9/45 · repo 2 11/60 · 3 unplanted · 56.4 min · $0.79 bill · `default` · Claude Code / OpenRouter · single run · 2026-09-22

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-22 by the first-party row (18/105, effort explicitly asserted). This row ran at `default` over an aggregator hop - the aggregator's reasoning dial is accepted but measured inert, so `default` never named a state that could be asked for again. The first-party row asserts the model's one real toggle (`thinking.type=enabled`) explicitly and drops the aggregator hop entirely; it scored 2 points lower (18 vs 20), inside the noise this board has already measured elsewhere (widest observed spread: 6 of 105) and expected here since both rows almost certainly ran with reasoning already on. Nothing here is retracted - the 20 happened and this is its receipt - but it is no longer the row that best answers whether reasoning is on for this model. The OpenRouter route itself was also independently replicated: see 'MiMo-V2.6-Pro - mean of 2' (mean of two runs, 22.0 of 105) for the same-route, same-configuration successor. This row is superseded on two separate axes - route (the first-party row) and sample size (the mean row) - neither displacing the other.

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean. Models on this board have moved several points between runs of an identical configuration - the widest observed spread is 6 of 105 - so read this number with that much slack in either direction. Effort is `default`: the route's `reasoning` field is accepted but measured inert (n=3 per condition, 2026-09-22), so a dial does exist here, contrary to what an earlier version of this row said, but it does not change behaviour - this is still not a measurement of the model at any particular reasoning level. The wall figure is contended: other vendors' legs shared the machine.

Xiaomi's MiMo-V2.6-Pro driven by Claude Code through an Anthropic-API shim onto OpenRouter. 20 of 105: 9 of 45 on repo 1 and 11 of 60 on repo 2, 3 genuine extras, 56.4 minutes, $0.79. ONE RUN - a first measurement of a model nothing on this board has seen, published as a single run and labelled as one. *** CORRECTED 2026-09-22: THE DIAL EXISTS, IT IS JUST INERT *** This endpoint's supported_parameters do include `reasoning` (frequency_penalty, include_reasoning, max_tokens, presence_penalty, reasoning, response_format, stop, structured_outputs, temperature, tool_choice, tools and top_p) - an earlier version of this note read that list as proof no dial exists at all, which overstated it. `reasoning` is exactly the field OpenRouter's {effort: low|medium|high} convention uses, and this endpoint accepts it. A direct probe against the same pinned host (n=3 per condition, same prompt, no-field vs effort=low vs effort=high) found reasoning-token counts that fully overlap: 738-1704 no-field, 739-997 low, 814-1023 high, no ordering between low and high. Same failure mode already measured on Qwen3.8-Max on this board: accepted, not binding. The row is `default` because that is what ran, before this probe existed - there was a dial to test, and it does nothing. *** SINGLE FIRST-PARTY HOST, PINNED ANYWAY *** OpenRouter's endpoint API lists one host for this slug: Xiaomi's own, tag xiaomi/fp8, ctx 1,048,576 and max output 131,072. The provider pin with allow_fallbacks=false is set regardless, because a slug served by one host at launch is not a slug served by one host for the life of a run, and an unpinned row that silently becomes mixed-path averages hosts instead of measuring a model. The CLI model string carries [1m] because the upstream context is 1M and that suffix is the only context hint the CLI accepts - without it the CLI assumes 200K and thrashes on autocompact. *** COST IS A REAL BILL *** $0.79 is the OpenRouter credits delta across both legs, not a token estimate, which makes it directly comparable to the other billed rows here and not to the list-priced or floor-reconstructed ones. List rates for context: 0.435 in / 0.0036 cache-read / 0.87 out per MTok. *** RECEIPTS *** Both legs exited 0, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. The run shared the laptop with benchmark legs from other vendors, which the wall marker on this row discloses - scores, tokens and cost are untouched by that, only the clock. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Muse Spark 1.3 (xhigh effort, Muse Code, Meta API)

**20 / 105** · repo 1 8/45 · repo 2 12/60 · 7 unplanted · 73.0 min · $15.58 · `xhigh` · Muse Code / Meta API · single run · 2026-09-13

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-14 by the n=3 MEAN row at the same rung (20.3, rounding to 20). This row is one of three runs that scored 20, 25 and 16; it is kept because a single run is no longer how this rung is reported, and nothing in it is corrected by arithmetic. Read the mean row's range - 9 points - before quoting anything this row says about a 1-point gap to high.

**Caveat.** One run at this tier, as at each of the others, so the curve is three points of n=1. What it gains over the pair it replaces is a near-repeat: high and xhigh are indistinguishable by every measurement available, they were run hours apart on the same stack, and they landed at 19 and 20. That is the closest thing to a run-to-run spread estimate this dial has, and a 13-point gap to max clears it comfortably. It is still not a replicate at max, which is the leg that would settle it. WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED: that xhigh is broken or ignored. It is accepted by the endpoint, echoed back applied, sits inside a dial measured real at the bottom, and on repo 1 it did think 54% harder than high. It simply did not convert that into fixes. Nor is it claimed that the ordering is exact - 19 against 20 is not evidence that xhigh beats high. THE HARNESS VERSION IS CAPTURED, NOT TYPED: this row records the muse --version read at run time, because muse self-updates and shipped a wrong version string to this board once already. THE SHIM IN THE PATH IS A METER AND NOT A ROUTE: Muse Code's JSON stream carries no usage payloads at all, so without it this row would have no billing record; pointed at Meta with the model rewrite and the provider pin both OFF it forwards the turn untouched and only counts. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: a subscription account exposes no per-run credits delta, so this is costed from the run's own token counts at the same Meta list rates the OpenRouter row was billed at, which keeps the Muse Code rows comparable. The benchmark repos were re-installed from the same lockfile inside Linux before the agent started, because the Windows install lays down win32 binaries Linux node cannot execute.

The third and last rung of Meta's first-party effort dial to be run on this board, and the one that turns a pair into a curve. *** THE WHOLE VALUE OF THIS DIAL SITS IN THE FINAL RUNG *** Three legs on one stack, one variable apart: high scores 19 of 105, xhigh scores 20, max scores 33. What is paid for them rises smoothly and the score does not: list cost runs 10.81, 15.58, 19.83 dollars and reasoning tokens run 178,300 then 225,766 then 286,242 - a step of almost exactly 27% at each rung. The first 27% more thinking buys ONE POINT, which is inside anything this board would call noise. The second 27% buys THIRTEEN. Going from high to xhigh costs 44% more and changes nothing measurable; going on to max costs another 27% and is the largest effort effect on this board. *** AND IT FALSIFIES THIS BOARD'S OWN MAGNITUDE RECEIPT A SECOND TIME, MORE PRECISELY THAN THE FIRST *** Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260913-musespark13-meta-effort-magnitude.txt measured thinking volume at n=3 on one fixed prompt, found high, xhigh and max overlapping, and concluded they were a single saturated rung where paying for max over high buys nothing measurable. The bench says the top of this dial is TWO rungs, not one: high and xhigh land within a point of each other, and max is thirteen above both. So the probe got half of it right - high and xhigh ARE one rung, on the score as well as on the tokens - and put max in the wrong group, which is the half a buyer pays for. A token-volume sweep can group tiers and cannot price them. *** ON THE REAL WORKLOAD xhigh DOES NOT RELIABLY THINK MORE THAN high EITHER *** Per repo the sign flips: repo 1 at xhigh spent 178,161 reasoning tokens against high's 115,670, up 54%, while repo 2 at xhigh spent 47,605 against high's 62,630, down 24%. The combined +27% is one repo carrying it. So no measurement this board holds - toy-prompt tokens, bench tokens, or bench score - separates xhigh from high, and this row is published as the evidence for that rather than against it. *** THE EFFORT LABEL DESCRIBES THE MAIN LOOP, NOT THE RUN *** Muse Code fires a reminder-observer side agent at a fixed low/high/medium the flag does not reach, so on a three-step probe roughly 4 of 10 calls carried the asked tier. The tier is asserted, validated in two layers and echoed back applied for the main loop, which is the agent that does the work. Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260912-musecode-cli-effort-vs-wire.txt. Honesty profile: 8 of 45 on repo 1 and 12 of 60 on repo 2 with NO PARTIALS ON EITHER REPO - the first Muse row to manage that - ZERO claimed-only for the fifth Meta row running, and 7 genuine unplanted extras. Cost per strict fix is 78 cents here against 57 at high and 60 at max, which makes xhigh the worst-value rung of the three by the only metric that combines both columns.

## GPT-6 Luna (max effort) - mean of 2

**19 / 105** · repo 1 6.5/45 · repo 2 12.5/60 · 20.5 unplanted · 94.9 min · $0.51 · `max` · Codex CLI · mean of 2 runs · 2026-09-23

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-23 by the mean of 3 (18.3 of 105). This row reported 19.0 of 105 as the mean of 2 runs. The replicates are byte-identical re-runs; across 3 the runs span 17 to 21. Nothing here is retracted - it is kept as the receipt for what the board showed before the extra run.

**Caveat.** Two runs of an identical configuration scoring 17 and 21 of 105 - spread 4. Read the mean with that width, not as a point, and note that two runs cannot bound variance well - the third is still to come. It also runs a Codex build one version newer than the GPT-5.6 rows here, because the build they use refuses this model; it compares cleanly to the GPT-6 Sol rows, which run the same build. Cost is a list-rate estimate and a floor - the long-context surcharge is not modelled. The machine was idle for every leg.

OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna at max effort through OpenAI's own Codex CLI, on a ChatGPT account - the same auth path as every other Sol, Astra, Terra and Luna row here, NOT the metered API. Published as the MEAN OF TWO INDEPENDENT RUNS. Runs scored 17 and 21 of 105 (repo 1: 6, 7 of 45 - repo 2: 11, 14 of 60). Mean 19.0, spread 4. *** THIS SUPERSEDES GPT-6 LUNA (MAX EFFORT) (17 OF 105) *** *** WHAT THIS ROW IS FOR: HOW MUCH SCORE SURVIVES A 20x PRICE CUT *** list price is $0.10 in and $0.50 out per million, twenty times cheaper than GPT-6 Sol ($2 / $10) on both sides of the meter. The board publishes GPT-6 Astra at 45 (mean of 3) of 105 at max, GPT-6 Sol at 29.3 (mean of 3), and this model's own direct predecessor GPT-5.6 Luna at 31.3 (mean of 3). This row lands at 19.0. Read the gap against the price, not on its own. *** THE REPLICATES ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE FIRST RUN *** same model string gpt-6-luna, same effort, same timeout, same pinned executable, same code-mode setting, same price table, same blind judge. Only the arm name differs, so the spread above is run-to-run variance and nothing else. *** EXTRAS *** 22 and 19 genuine extras across the runs, mean 20.5 - real defects in the repos that are not part of the planted set. Extras are reported separately and never folded into the score. *** EXISTENCE WAS PROVED POSITIVELY, NOT INFERRED FROM A REJECTION *** the slug gpt-6-luna appears in the account's own model list and the pinned CLI accepted it and answered. The CLI returns 'The <slug> model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account' word for word for a deliberately fake slug, so a rejection is evidence about the client and never about whether a model exists. *** THIS ROW RUNS A DIFFERENT CLI BUILD FROM THE OLDER OPENAI ROWS, UNAVOIDABLY *** the Codex build the rest of this board uses (0.154.0) refuses this model outright. 0.155.1 runs it on the same account, same auth, same machine, so the blocker was the client and not entitlement. There is no version of this measurement that holds the harness fixed against the GPT-5.6 rows. It DOES compare cleanly to the GPT-6 Sol rows, which run the same pinned 0.155.1. *** WALL AND COST *** Walls 85.8, 104.0 minutes, mean 94.9; cost $0.52, $0.49, mean $0.51. COST IS A LIST ESTIMATE AND A FLOOR - token counts at OpenAI's published short-context rates, $0.10 input, $0.01 cached, $0.50 output per million. Requests over 272K input bill 2x on input and cache and 1.5x on output, which is NOT modelled here, so any leg that crossed that line cost more than this figure says. The Codex CLI on a subscription exposes no per-run bill, hence `list`, comparable only to other list rows. *** THE JUDGE IS NOT THIS BOARD'S DEFAULT, BY RULE *** a model may never judge itself or a sibling, and the default Codex judge is an OpenAI model, so it is disqualified for this arm - as is every other first-party GPT checkpoint here, since the rule is about shared weights and not identity-knowledge. Judged by grok-4.7, blinded to the arm's identity - the id is read back from the scoring receipt, not the judge config, because the endpoint has resolved a request for one grok checkpoint to another. *** RECEIPTS *** All legs exited 0 with a report written, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. The machine was otherwise idle for every leg, so the wall figures compare directly against other uncontended rows.

## GLM-5.3 Flash (max effort, Z.ai API)

**19 / 105** · repo 1 10/45 · repo 2 9/60 · 4 unplanted · 58.4 min · $0.94 · `max` · Claude Code / Z.ai API · single run · 2026-09-12

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-15 by the n=3 MEAN row at the same rung (17.7/105). This row is ONE run of three and it is the HIGHEST of the three: identical runs scored 19, 18 and 16. Nothing in it is retracted; it was the top of its own range. The replication could not be VERIFIED - Z.ai reports no token accounting - so the mean that replaces it is three runs believed to match rather than confirmed to.

GLM-5.3 Flash on Z.ai's OWN Anthropic-compatible endpoint - no aggregator, no shim, the same first-party path as the full-size GLM-5.3 row, so the two sizes differ by model and by nothing else. It scores 19 of 105 for 95 cents in 58 minutes, which is the same 19 the full-size model scores on the same endpoint at the same asserted tier, for a sixteenth of the money. *** THIS NOTE TOLD READERS NOT TO CREDIT THE EFFORT DIAL. IT IS ANSWERED, 2026-09-13, AND THE DIAL GETS THE CREDIT *** A leg at low on this exact path scores 9 of 105 against this row's 19, with route, harness, model, prompt and repos all held fixed - so THE TIER IS WORTH TEN POINTS ON THIS MODEL and the OpenRouter row's 13 sits inside the range the dial alone covers. Superseded wording, kept verbatim rather than deleted: do not read the jump from the OpenRouter Flash row as an effort result; Flash scored 13 through OpenRouter and 19 here, and the tempting story is that the tier now binds; the control on this board argues against it, because the full-size model scored 19 on BOTH routes, so the same change of route and tier moved it by zero; each of these is one run and a 6-point move on n=1 does not clear the run-to-run spread measured elsewhere. THE CONTROL ITSELF IS NOT RETRACTED: the full-size GLM-5.3 has no tier pair on this board, only a route pair, so the zero it moved is a statement about its ROUTE and says nothing about its dial, which nobody has run. What was wrong was generalising one model's route result onto another model's tier. *** EFFORT max IS A SETTING, NOT A REQUEST *** Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260912-glm53flash-zai-anthropic.txt. The endpoint validates output_config.effort - the field Claude Code actually puts on the wire - against a CLOSED enum: low, high and max return 200 while none, minimal, medium, xhigh and an invented bogus_zzz are all rejected HTTP 400 code 1210, and a bad model id is rejected separately as 1211, so the effort rejection is about the field rather than about a malformed request. An endpoint that refuses everything outside its list is not silently clamping what is inside it. Identical accept-list on both GLM sizes. NOTE THE FIELD, because it nearly produced the opposite verdict: a first pass probed top-level reasoning_effort, which is Z.ai's own OpenAI-compatible field name, got HTTP 200 on bogus_zzz and would have published this path as inert. output_config is what the endpoint reads and what the CLI sends. max is also Z.ai's documented default for this family, so a default run and a max run here are the same run - the row asserts the tier rather than relying on that. WHY THIS ROW IS LATE: the 2026-09-10 first-party pass tried Flash first and got HTTP 429 on this key, so it was written against the full-size model and Flash kept its aggregator row. The 429 is gone. *** AND THE LABEL MOVES WITH THE EVIDENCE: first_party -> verified_ceiling *** It shipped as first_party because the accept-list proved only that the endpoint READS the field. The low leg separates the levels on this serving path - 10 points of score and 3.1x the output tokens - which is what verified requires, and the same accept-list makes max the top rung that exists, which is what promotes it to a ceiling. Honesty profile: 10 of 45 on repo 1 with ONE claimed-only, 9 of 60 plus 2 partials on repo 2 with none, and 4 genuine extras across both. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: Z.ai publishes no balance or usage endpoint on this plan key, so no credits delta exists to read; costed at the same published Flash rates the OpenRouter Flash row was billed at, which keeps the two Flash rows comparable - those rates carry a launch discount that can lapse. ctx 1M, model string [1m].

What the pair shows is that on THIS stack the dial is worth paying for, which is the opposite of the Qwen and GLM findings and is why both belong on the same page. THE HARNESS VERSION IS CAPTURED, NOT TYPED: this row records the muse --version read at run time, because muse self-updates and shipped a wrong version string to this board once already. THE SHIM IN THE PATH IS A METER AND NOT A ROUTE: Muse Code's JSON stream carries no usage payloads at all, so without it this row would have no billing record; pointed at Meta with the model rewrite and the provider pin both OFF it forwards the turn untouched and only counts. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: a subscription account exposes no per-run credits delta, so this is costed from the run's own token counts at the same Meta list rates the OpenRouter row was billed at, which keeps the Muse Code rows comparable. The benchmark repos were re-installed from the same lockfile inside Linux before the agent started, because the Windows install lays down win32 binaries Linux node cannot execute.

Meta's own agent on Meta's own endpoint at high - which is also this endpoint's MEASURED default, since omitting the effort field returns an echo of high. So this row is what the stack does out of the box first-party, and it exists to answer a question the max row could not. *** IT SETTLES THE ATTRIBUTION, AND AGAINST THE READING THIS BOARD PUBLISHED FIRST *** The max row scored 33/105 against an OpenRouter sibling's 18 with the ROUTE and the EFFORT having moved together, and its note warned against crediting the dial. The sibling is now known to have run at high as well - an unflagged Muse Code sends reasoning.effort=high and that route applies it - so these three rows differ one variable at a time: OpenRouter at high scores 18, Meta first-party at high scores 19, Meta first-party at max scores 33. THE ROUTE IS WORTH ABOUT A POINT. THE TIER IS WORTH FOURTEEN. That is the largest effect an effort setting has produced on this board, and it is the reverse of what the GLM-5.3 first-party pair showed, where the same kind of move was worth zero. *** AND IT FALSIFIES A PREDICTION THIS BOARD PUBLISHED IN WRITING *** Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260913-musespark13-meta-effort-magnitude.txt said that because high and max could not be separated in thinking tokens at n=3 on a fixed prompt (means 9385 against 12655, ranges overlapping), a bench leg at high should land near 33. It landed at 19. On the real workload the tiers are not close: this row spent 178,300 reasoning tokens against the max row's 286,242, a 61% gap the toy prompt could not see. READ THAT AS A LIMIT ON MAGNITUDE PROBES GENERALLY - a token-volume sweep shows whether a dial is CONNECTED, not what it is WORTH, and this board will not treat one as evidence for the other again. *** THE EFFORT LABEL DESCRIBES THE MAIN LOOP, NOT THE RUN *** Muse Code fires a reminder-observer side agent at a fixed low/high/medium the flag does not reach, so on a three-step probe roughly 4 of 10 calls carried the asked tier. The tier is asserted, validated in two layers and echoed back applied for the main loop, which is the agent that does the work. Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260912-musecode-cli-effort-vs-wire.txt. Honesty profile: 8 of 45 on repo 1 with no partials, 11 of 60 plus 2 partials on repo 2, ZERO claimed-only on either repo - the fourth Meta row in a row with a clean claim record - and 10 genuine unplanted extras.

## Grok 4.7 (high)

**19 / 105** · repo 1 9/45 · repo 2 10/60 · 18 unplanted · 40.5 min · $11.53 floor · `high` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · single run · 2026-09-21

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-21 by the n=3 row at the same configuration (19.3 of 105). The three runs scored 19, 22 and 17, so this single draw at 19 sat inside a 5-point spread it could not show. Nothing it claimed is retracted; it was published as an n=1 row and read as one. Kept rather than deleted because it is the receipt for what the board showed for part of 2026-09-21.

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean. The same model at its top rung spanned 25 to 31 of 105 across three runs, so read this number with several points of slack in either direction. Cost is a reconstructed FLOOR rather than a bill, and it uses a per-session arithmetic the older grok rows on this board do not yet use; the note gives both figures.

Grok 4.7 at high through xAI's own CLI - the CLI DEFAULT rung - what someone gets who never touches the effort setting, and the only number on this dial that describes the out-of-the-box experience. 19 of 105: 9 of 45 on repo 1 and 10 of 60 on repo 2, 18 genuine extras, 40.5 minutes, a reconstructed floor of $11.53. ONE RUN. *** AGAINST THE PREVIOUS GENERATION AT THE SAME RUNG *** Grok 4.6 (high) scored 23.0 of 105 (7 on repo 1, 16 on repo 2), also a single run, for a difference of -4. Both sides are n=1, so this is one draw against one draw. The scale of the doubt is not a guess: three runs of the 4.6 configuration one rung up spanned 27 to 30 of 105, and three runs of this model at that rung spanned 25 to 31 - so a gap of a few points between two single runs is inside what the same configuration produces run to run. *** WHERE IT SITS ON ITS OWN DIAL *** This model at xhigh is 28.8 of 105, published as a mean of four. This rung is 19, a single run. The 4.6 dial for comparison ran low 15, medium 23, high 23, xhigh 28.7 - FLAT between medium and high, which is the most interesting thing on it, and the low rung is where that dial had real room. Whether either shape repeats here is a question the rungs answer together: read this row beside its siblings rather than on its own. *** THE TIER WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED, AND FOR THE LOWER RUNGS THAT MATTERS MORE *** A zero-token ACP readback before launch returned reasoning_effort high for a requested high. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string AND its default is `high`, so a rung that failed to apply would look identical to one that worked in every artefact except this readback - the run would simply be a default-effort run wearing another label. The enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh. The served model is named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. *** COST IS A FLOOR *** The grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates, the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis. This row uses the per-session arithmetic corrected on 2026-09-21; on the older run-level arithmetic the other grok rows still use it would read $11.54. It is a floor twice over: xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates. *** RECEIPTS *** Both legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL, one repo at a time, uncontended - nothing else ran alongside. The agent spawned 1 session(s) on repo 1 and 1 on repo 2 (57 and 73 inference requests); that count varies run to run on identical arm definitions for this model, and it tracks cost far more closely than score. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Qwen3.8-Max

**19 / 105** · repo 1 5/45 · repo 2 14/60 · 6 unplanted · 148.2 min · $33.30 · `default` · Claude Code / OpenRouter · single run · 2026-08-03

**Superseded.** Superseded by the Sep 11 first-party run: same model on Alibaba's own endpoint instead of Claude Code -> local proxy -> OpenRouter, scoring 28/105 for $26.29 against this row's 19/105 for $33.30. This row billed 12.94% of its prompt tokens as uncached input against the first-party run's 0.0019%, so it priced the route rather than the model.

CORRECTED 2026-09-12 (4): EFFORT IS INERT ON THIS ROW, measured at last. It shipped verified_ceiling, was corrected to first_party on 09-11, and is now inert_default with effort 'default' - the same label every other aggregator-routed row on this board carries. A nine-runs-per-condition probe on the exact surface this arm used (OpenRouter's Anthropic-compatible /v1/messages, receipts results/effort-dial-probes/20260912-qwen38max-openrouter-anthropic.txt and 20260912-qwen38max-all-n9.txt) puts reasoning.effort=low at mean 13,017 output tokens (7,897-16,402), reasoning.effort=high at 14,815 (11,524-20,237), and NO effort field at all at 13,622 (9,179-19,757) - a 1.14x spread between the tiers against a 2.15x worst within-condition spread, with every range overlapping every other. The field does nothing; the run sat at Alibaba's own default either way. SELF-CORRECTION, same day: for a few hours this note also said the shim had caused the problem by deleting Anthropic's native 'thinking' field to install 'reasoning.effort', on the strength of that field measuring very differently (mean 5,468). That rested on ONE budget value, which can only show a field is read, not that it binds. A three-value probe (receipt results/effort-dial-probes/20260912-qwen38max-budget-scaling-openrouter.txt, n=3 each) put budget_tokens=2,000 at mean 5,627 output tokens, 8,000 at 4,735 and 24,000 at 4,111 - twelve times the budget moved the mean 0.73x, the wrong way, inside a 1.95x worst within-condition spread. So budget_tokens is a switch rather than a dial on this path, and the switch SUPPRESSES: sending it in any form costs about two thirds of the model's thinking against the 13,622 no-field baseline. That makes the shim's pop the configuration producing the MOST thinking available here, not a discarded control - with 'thinking' gone and reasoning.effort inert, the request fell through to the endpoint default, which is exactly what inert_default records. The label is unaffected; only the causal story changed, and the shims were reverted to dropping the field. A control on Alibaba's own endpoint shows native thinking landing far higher there and fully overlapping Alibaba's own no-field baseline, so the suppression is a property of the aggregator hop and not of the model. RE-MEASURED AT n=6 AND THEN n=9 the same day, because a claim this size should not rest on three samples (receipt results/effort-dial-probes/20260912-qwen38max-all-n9.txt, 24 fresh concurrent calls pooled with the originals and the n=6 top-ups; every condition on both routes now stands at nine runs, so no verdict rests on a smaller sample than the one it is compared against): through OpenRouter budget_tokens=24,000 gives mean 4,967 (3,568-7,637) against a no-field baseline of 13,622 (9,179-19,757); straight to Alibaba the same field gives 13,168 (9,185-15,977) against 14,379 (11,586-17,442). All verdicts survive - cross-route 2.65x against a 2.15x worst within-cell spread, ranges disjoint, the field costing 64% of the thinking through the hop and nothing at all direct. The effort WORD reads more inert at nine runs than at three: low-vs-high means 1.14x through OpenRouter and 1.04x direct, ranges fully overlapping. The bigger samples did move one number: the OpenRouter no-field baseline measured 15,714 at n=3, 13,339 at n=6 and 13,622 at n=9 - three samples had overestimated it by 15%, and the six- and nine-run readings agree to 2%. Quote the nine-run figures only. Ranges can only widen with n, so each pass was a test the finding could fail. Still at n=3 and flagged as such: the three-value budget sweep, whose top-up was attempted the same day and blocked when the OpenRouter account ran out of credits mid-series - read the RANKING of the three budgets as unmeasured; the suppression claim does not depend on it. The scores, cost and wall are unchanged. EARLIER CORRECTIONS, 2026-09-11, on three counts, while adding the Qwen3.8-Flash rows. (1) HARNESS. This row was published as 'Claude Code / Alibaba API'. It never called Alibaba's API. The arm ran through a local pass-through shim with upstream=OpenRouter's qwen3.8-max slug, so the path was Claude Code -> local proxy -> OpenRouter -> Alibaba, exactly like the Kimi K3, Ox Alpha and GLM-5.3 rows, all of which correctly say 'Claude Code / OpenRouter'. This row was the only OR-routed row on the board claiming a direct vendor API. (2) EFFORT STATUS. It was published verified_ceiling. No probe receipt exists - the only Qwen file in results/effort-dial-probes is the 2026-09-11 Flash one. The claim rested on QwenCloud's documented enum (low|medium|xhigh, default xhigh, high->xhigh) plus a check that thinking was live (248 thinking blocks across the raw logs). Under this board's own vocabulary that is first_party - a documented tier taken at face value - because verified_ceiling requires a probe that SEPARATES the levels. The row's own note also argued that Alibaba validates effort 'while OR accepts any string and applies none', which cannot both be true of a request routed through OR; whether the effort field survived the proxy and the aggregator to reach Alibaba was never established. (3) COST, $31.10 -> $33.30. The arm modelled cache writes as FREE. OpenRouter lists qwen3.8-max-0902 cache writes at $2.50/MTok and this run wrote 879,902 cache-creation tokens. A sweep of OR's listing found Qwen is the ONLY family on this board it charges a cache-write fee for - DeepSeek, Z.ai, Moonshot, Tencent and Meta publish none - so $0.00 is right on every other aggregator-routed arm and was wrong only here. The SCORES are unchanged; only the two labels and the cost move. And a caveat larger than the correction: 12.94% of this row's prompt tokens (8,282,777 of them, $16.57 - HALF the row) were billed as uncached input, against 0.002% on the two Flash rows running the same harness against Alibaba's own endpoint. That ~5,000x gap in cache-miss rate is a property of the proxy-plus-aggregator path, not of the model, so read this row's cost as a cost OF THAT PATH rather than a price for Qwen3.8-Max. NOT re-probed on purpose: the Aug 3 arm used an unlisted qwen3.8-max slug and what OpenRouter lists now is qwen3.8-max-0902, a dated snapshot, so a probe today would measure a different serving path than this row shipped on. The 2026-09-11 Flash finding does not transfer either (different model AND different path).

## Muse Spark 1.3 (high effort, Muse Code, OpenRouter)

**18 / 105** · repo 1 6/45 · repo 2 12/60 · 17 unplanted · 53.3 min · $9.53 bill · `high` · Muse Code / OpenRouter · single run · 2026-09-12

**Superseded.** Superseded by the Meta first-party row at the SAME TIER (19/105). Both ran Muse Code at high on Muse Spark 1.3; this one reached the model through OpenRouter and that one through api.meta.ai directly, so the pair is a clean route comparison and the first-party leg is the one to quote. The route is worth about a point - 18 against 19 - which is the useful thing this row establishes on its way out. It is kept rather than deleted because it is the only REAL BILL on a Muse Code row: the first-party rows run on a subscription account that exposes no per-run credits delta, so their cost columns are list estimates and this one is not.

**Caveat.** One run, at high. THE ORIGINAL CAVEAT HERE CLAIMED THE OPPOSITE AND IS RETRACTED: it said the aggregator hop was PROVEN inert, that Muse Code sends high and OpenRouter drops it, and that four receipts at n=9 showed every tier overlapping with bogus_zzz returning HTTP 200. Every one of those receipts probed api/v1/MESSAGES with a flat top-level reasoning_effort, which is not the surface this harness uses; on api/v1/RESPONSES, where the shim actually forwards, the tiers separate, an invented tier is refused 400, and the field is applied rather than dropped. Muse Code does send high - that part was right - and the route honours it, which is why this row now reads high/verified. The 402 that forced this row onto an aggregator is unrelated and still true. The route is an aggregator because Meta's own endpoint answers 402 here, billing verification failed, which is also why cost is a REAL OpenRouter credits delta rather than a list estimate. The benchmark repos were re-installed from the same lockfile inside Linux before the agent started, because the Windows install lays down win32 binaries Linux node cannot execute and an agent that cannot run the suite is solving a different task than every other row.

The first row on this board driven by a vendor's OWN agent rather than by Claude Code. *** ITS EFFORT LABEL WAS WRONG WHEN PUBLISHED AND IS CORRECTED HERE (2026-09-13) *** It shipped as default/inert_default on the strength of four receipts showing every tier overlapping on OpenRouter. Those receipts probed api/v1/MESSAGES with a flat top-level reasoning_effort. Muse Code speaks the RESPONSES API and the shim forwards to api/v1/RESPONSES, touching only the model id, the provider pin and the stream flag. Re-probed on THAT surface with the nested reasoning:{effort} the harness actually sends, at n=3 per tier on the same prompt as the Meta first-party probe, the dial is not inert: mean reasoning tokens run 845, 2644, 5111, 7858, 10114 and 13243 across minimal to max - a 15.7x spread against a 1.46x worst within-tier spread, with minimal, low, medium and high mutually disjoint - an invented tier is refused HTTP 400, and an omitted field echoes medium. AND THE ARM NEVER USED THAT DEFAULT: captured at the request body, an unflagged Muse Code sends reasoning.effort=high on its main loop rather than omitting the field, so this row ran at high and is relabelled high/verified. The score, cost, wall and extras are unchanged - only the label was ever wrong. Receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260913-musespark13-openrouter-responses-magnitude.txt, and the four superseded receipts carry a correction banner rather than being deleted, because their finding stands for the surface they measured. Muse Code 0.1.0 runs the loop, picks the tools and decides when to stop, and Meta's model answers it - the same experiment the Kimi CLI row runs for Moonshot. *** DO NOT READ THIS AS A HARNESS A/B AGAINST THE OTHER META ROW *** The board's only other Meta row is Muse Spark 1.2 in Claude Code, on Meta's own endpoint, at a genuine xhigh (17/105). Against this row the MODEL VERSION, the HARNESS, the ROUTE and the EFFORT all differ, so the pair is a comparison of two stacks as shipped and not an isolation of any one of them. Muse Code is chattier than a single agent loop by design: it runs a reminder-observer side-agent alongside the main one, so the turn count and the token bill both include work no Claude Code row does. *** WHY THIS ROW NEEDED TWO HARNESS FIXES BEFORE IT COULD EXIST *** Neither is a property of the model and both are disclosed because they changed what ran. First, the launcher that starts the Meta-API shim inside WSL had never once worked - it backgrounded the shim inside a session that is torn down the instant the launching shell exits, so the shim died before it could create its own log file. Second, and less visibly, the shim was not asking upstream for a stream: Muse Code requests one with an Accept header, OpenRouter reads the body, so every turn arrived as a single blob when generation finished - and Muse Code kills a run whose model stream has been idle for 180 seconds, on a route that routinely takes 200 to 230 seconds per call. The first attempt at this arm was billed for a response it never saw. Both legs of this row ran with both fixes in place.

## GPT-6 Luna (max effort)

**17 / 105** · repo 1 6/45 · repo 2 11/60 · 22 unplanted · 85.8 min · $0.52 · `max` · Codex CLI · single run · 2026-09-23

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-23 by the mean of 2 (19.0 of 105). This row reported 17 of 105 from a single run, published the day the arm was added. The replicates are byte-identical re-runs; across 2 the runs span 17 to 21. Nothing here is retracted - it is kept as the receipt for what the board showed before the extra run.

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean - and this board has measured a 13-point spread between two runs of a single identical max-effort configuration, so treat this as one draw. Two replicates are queued and will supersede it. Runs a Codex build one version newer than the GPT-5.6 rows here, because the build they use refuses this model; it compares cleanly to the GPT-6 Sol rows, which run the same build. Cost is a list-rate estimate and a floor - the long-context surcharge is not modelled. The machine was idle for both legs.

OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna at max effort through OpenAI's own Codex CLI, on a ChatGPT account - the same auth path as every other Sol, Astra, Terra and Luna row here, NOT the metered API. 17 of 105: 6 of 45 on repo 1 and 11 of 60 on repo 2, 22 genuine extras, 85.8 minutes, $0.52. ONE RUN so far; two replicates are queued and will supersede this row. *** THIS IS THE CHEAP END OF THE GPT-6 FAMILY *** list price is $0.10 in and $0.50 out per million, TWENTY TIMES cheaper than GPT-6 Sol ($2 / $10) on both sides of the meter. So the question this row answers is not whether it wins - it is how much of the family's score survives a 20x price cut. For the comparison the board publishes GPT-6 Astra at 45 (mean of 3) of 105 at max, GPT-6 Sol at 29.3 (mean of 3), and this model's own direct predecessor GPT-5.6 Luna at 31.3 (mean of 3). *** ONE RUN IS ONE DRAW *** this board has measured a 13-point spread between two runs of a single identical max-effort configuration. Read this figure, and any gap between it and a peer, with that width in mind until the replicates land. *** EXISTENCE WAS PROVED POSITIVELY, NOT INFERRED FROM A REJECTION *** the slug gpt-6-luna appears in the account's own model list and the pinned CLI accepted it and answered. That distinction matters on this board: the CLI returns 'The <slug> model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account' word for word for a deliberately fake slug, so a rejection is evidence about the client and never about whether a model exists. *** THIS ROW RUNS A DIFFERENT CLI BUILD FROM THE OLDER OPENAI ROWS, UNAVOIDABLY *** the Codex build the rest of this board uses (0.154.0) refuses this model outright. 0.155.1 runs it on the same account, same auth, same machine, so the blocker was the client and not entitlement. There is no version of this measurement that holds the harness fixed against the GPT-5.6 rows. It DOES compare cleanly to the GPT-6 Sol rows, which run the same pinned 0.155.1. *** COST IS A LIST ESTIMATE AND A FLOOR *** token counts at OpenAI's published short-context rates, $0.10 input, $0.01 cached, $0.50 output per million. Requests over 272K input bill 2x on input and cache and 1.5x on output, which is NOT modelled here, so any leg that crossed that line cost more than this figure says. The Codex CLI on a subscription exposes no per-run bill, hence `list`, comparable only to other list rows. *** THE JUDGE IS NOT THIS BOARD'S DEFAULT, BY RULE *** a model may never judge itself or a sibling, and the default Codex judge is an OpenAI model, so it is disqualified for this arm - as is every other first-party GPT checkpoint here, since the rule is about shared weights and not identity-knowledge. Judged by grok-4.7, blinded to the arm's identity - the id is read back from the scoring receipt, not the judge config, because the endpoint has resolved a request for one grok checkpoint to another. *** RECEIPTS *** Both legs exited 0 with a report written, run SEQUENTIALLY, one repo at a time. The machine was otherwise idle for every leg, so the wall figures compare directly against other uncontended rows.

## Qwen3.8-27B (xhigh effort, Alibaba API)

**15 / 105** · repo 1 6/45 · repo 2 9/60 · 2 unplanted · 46.8 min · $6.09 · `xhigh` · Claude Code / Alibaba API · single run · 2026-09-12

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-15 by the n=3 MEAN row at the same rung (15.0/105, range 4). This row is one of three runs that scored 15, 13 and 17, and it landed exactly on the eventual mean - a coincidence of one draw, not a property of the config. Nothing here is retracted: the 15 happened and this is its receipt, and it happens to also be this rung's most-representative single number. Kept rather than deleted because a supersession is only legible next to the row it supersedes.

**Caveat.** One run, and the effort_status is first_party rather than verified for a specific reason: the MAGNITUDE of the dial was probed on this endpoint for the other two Qwen3.8 sizes (both bind hard at the bottom and saturate across high/xhigh/max) but not for this one, so what is verified here is that the field is accepted and validated, not that xhigh buys more thinking than high on this particular model. Given the shape the siblings show, expect it to buy little. Cost is a list-price ESTIMATE and not a bill - DashScope exposes no credits API on this path - computed from the run's own token counts at 0.425 in / 0.5312 cache-write / 0.085 cache-read / 2.55 out per MTok, from OpenRouter's Alibaba-endpoint listing for this slug; that source is trusted here because for Qwen3.8-Max it lists exactly the DashScope rates this board already carries. Cache writes are priced at 0.5312 and NOT zero: Qwen is the one family on this board that is charged for them, and modelling them free is the error the Aug 3 Qwen row shipped with. The model id was probed rather than assumed - the endpoint serves `qwen3.8-27b` and 400s on -instruct, -0902 and qwen3-8-27b.

The middle size of the Qwen3.8 family, on Alibaba's own Anthropic-compatible endpoint - the same first-party path as the Qwen3.8-Max and Qwen3.8-Flash rows, so the three sizes differ by model and by nothing else. It is the most interesting Qwen row on the board because it LOSES to both siblings and costs more than one of them: 15/105 for $6.09 against Flash's 26/105 for $1.81 and Max's 28/105 for $26.29. Eleven points below a model that costs a THIRD as much, on the same endpoint, in the same harness, on the same two repos. It is the fast one - 46.8 minutes against 97.3 and 103.4, less than half the wall clock of either sibling - so read it as a latency option and not a price/capability midpoint, because on capability per dollar there is no case for it here. *** THE EFFORT LABEL IS MEASURED, AND IT IS NOT WHAT WAS ASKED FOR *** This arm was commissioned at DEFAULT effort. On this route there is no such thing. A logging pass-through of the CLI (receipt: effort-dial-probes/20260912-claude-code-wire-default-effort.txt) shows an UNFLAGGED Claude Code putting output_config.effort=xhigh on every main call - Claude Code has no send-nothing mode - and this endpoint IMPLEMENTS that field: it 400-rejects bogus_zzz and names its accept-list (low, medium, high, xhigh, max), the same five the other two Qwen3.8 sizes expose. So an out-of-the-box run here is an xhigh run, the row says xhigh, and the arm passes --effort xhigh explicitly so the result does not depend on a CLI default that can change under it. An unrequested tier is not an absent tier. Honesty profile: 15 strict matches, ZERO partials, 4 claimed-only across both repos, 2 genuine unplanted extras and no false-positive fixes.

## Grok 4.7 (low)

**11 / 105** · repo 1 5/45 · repo 2 6/60 · 12 unplanted · 15.1 min · $5.41 floor · `low` · Grok Build CLI (ACP) · single run · 2026-09-21

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-21 by the n=3 row at the same configuration (15.7 of 105). The three runs scored 11, 21 and 15, so this single draw at 11 sat at the edge of a 10-point spread it could not show. Nothing it claimed is retracted; it was published as an n=1 row and read as one. Kept rather than deleted because it is the receipt for what the board showed for part of 2026-09-21.

**Caveat.** ONE RUN, not a mean. The same model at its top rung spanned 25 to 31 of 105 across three runs, so read this number with several points of slack in either direction. Cost is a reconstructed FLOOR rather than a bill, and it uses a per-session arithmetic the older grok rows on this board do not yet use; the note gives both figures.

Grok 4.7 at low through xAI's own CLI - the LOW rung, the floor of the effort range - and the rung where the 4.6 dial had the most room, scoring 15 of 105 against its own 28.7 at the ceiling. 11 of 105: 5 of 45 on repo 1 and 6 of 60 on repo 2, 12 genuine extras, 15.1 minutes, a reconstructed floor of $5.41. ONE RUN. *** AGAINST THE PREVIOUS GENERATION AT THE SAME RUNG *** Grok 4.6 (low) scored 15.0 of 105 (6 on repo 1, 9 on repo 2), also a single run, for a difference of -4. Both sides are n=1, so this is one draw against one draw. The scale of the doubt is not a guess: three runs of the 4.6 configuration one rung up spanned 27 to 30 of 105, and three runs of this model at that rung spanned 25 to 31 - so a gap of a few points between two single runs is inside what the same configuration produces run to run. *** WHERE IT SITS ON ITS OWN DIAL *** This model at xhigh is 28.8 of 105, published as a mean of four. This rung is 11, a single run. The 4.6 dial for comparison ran low 15, medium 23, high 23, xhigh 28.7 - FLAT between medium and high, which is the most interesting thing on it, and the low rung is where that dial had real room. Whether either shape repeats here is a question the rungs answer together: read this row beside its siblings rather than on its own. *** THE TIER WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED, AND FOR THE LOWER RUNGS THAT MATTERS MORE *** A zero-token ACP readback before launch returned reasoning_effort low for a requested low. The grok CLI silently clamps an unknown effort string AND its default is `high`, so a rung that failed to apply would look identical to one that worked in every artefact except this readback - the run would simply be a default-effort run wearing another label. The enum grok-4.7 advertises is low, medium, high, xhigh. The served model is named by the session itself (currentModelId grok-4.7) rather than inferred from the -m flag, which has been inert on this bridge since it was probed on 2026-09-04. *** COST IS A FLOOR *** The grok CLI reports context occupancy rather than cumulative usage, so every grok row here is reconstructed from the CLI's own session logs at xAI list rates, the same 2/0.5/6 per MTok basis. This row uses the per-session arithmetic corrected on 2026-09-21; on the older run-level arithmetic the other grok rows still use it would read $5.41. It is a floor twice over: xAI bills a request whose prompt reaches 200K at DOUBLE those rates. *** RECEIPTS *** Both legs exited 0. Legs SEQUENTIAL, one repo at a time, uncontended - nothing else ran alongside. The agent spawned 1 session(s) on repo 1 and 1 on repo 2 (43 and 46 inference requests); that count varies run to run on identical arm definitions for this model, and it tracks cost far more closely than score. Judge: blind Codex gpt-5.5, which is neither this model nor a sibling of it.

## Muse Spark 1.3 (medium effort, Muse Code, Meta API)

**9 / 105** · repo 1 4/45 · repo 2 5/60 · 8 unplanted · 61.7 min · $10.02 · `medium` · Muse Code / Meta API · single run · 2026-09-13

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-14 by the n=3 MEAN row at the same rung (13/105). This row is ONE run of three, and it is the lowest of the three: medium scored 9, 15 and 15 on identical runs, and low scored 12, 13 and 4. THREE CLAIMS IN THE NOTE BELOW ARE RETRACTED. (1) 'Nearly the same money for less than half the result' - at the mean it is 13 against high's 19 for 9.24 dollars against 10.81. Cheaper and worse, but not half. (2) '1 dollar 11 per fix, the worst buy by a factor of two' - the mean is 71 cents per fix, mid-pack. (3) 'Reasoning volume does not explain it, medium emitted MORE than high' - the medium mean is 149,769 reasoning tokens against high's 178,300, so that inversion was one verbose run, not a property of the tier. TWO CLAIMS SURVIVE. On repo 2 medium still costs MORE in absolute dollars than high at the mean, 5.05 against 3.98, for 8 of 60 against 11 of 60. And the pre-registered branch still stands: 13 is still well below 19, so the dial does move score at the bottom while high-to-xhigh stays dead in the middle - it fired on a 10-point margin and the real one is 6. One more artifact of n=1: this run paired against a single low run of 12 put low ABOVE medium, and at n=3 the means are low 9.7 and medium 13.0, so that inversion was noise. Single runs at the bottom of this dial disagree by up to 9 points of 105. Kept rather than deleted because it is the receipt for what this board published on 2026-09-13, and because a retraction is only legible next to the claim it retracts.

**Caveat.** One run at each of the four tiers, so every step on this dial is n=1 against n=1. Four rungs ordering correctly is more than any single pair would give, but a repeat at any rung could move it, and the 1-point high-to-xhigh step is inside the noise this board would not credit in either direction. THE 10-POINT GAP TO high IS THE LARGEST RELATIVE STEP ON THIS DIAL, NOT THE LARGEST ABSOLUTE ONE - said precisely because the difference matters. Score more than doubles across this one notch, 2.11 times, against 1.65 times for xhigh to max; in raw points xhigh to max is the bigger move, 13 against 10. What makes this the step worth trusting is not its size but that it REPRODUCES ON BOTH REPOS INDEPENDENTLY - 8 to 4 on repo 1, 11 to 5 on repo 2, each one roughly halving. A gap that appears twice on two unrelated codebases is not a coin flip. WALL CLOCK ON THIS ROW CARRIES THROTTLING THE OTHER RUNGS DID NOT SEE. The shim absorbed 19 upstream 429s across the two legs against 4 apiece on the high, xhigh and max arms. Retries are transparent to the model and cost wall clock rather than correctness, but 61.7 minutes here is not cleanly comparable with 64.5 at high for that reason, and the comparison this row actually rests on is cost and score, not wall. THE EFFORT LABEL DESCRIBES THE MAIN LOOP, NOT EVERY CALL THIS ROW IS BILLED FOR. Muse Code runs a reminder-observer side agent at fixed efforts the flag does not control, so a share of these calls carried a tier nobody asked for - the same caveat every Muse Code row on this board carries, and the reason the dial is read on the main loop that does the work. billing=LIST ESTIMATE, NOT A BILL: this runs on a subscription account with no per-run credits delta to read; costed at the same Meta list rates as the other three first-party rows, which is what keeps the four comparable. ctx 1M.

The BOTTOM measured rung of Meta's first-party dial, and the row that makes this the worst-value dial on the board. It sits ONE NOTCH BELOW THIS ENDPOINT'S MEASURED DEFAULT: omitting reasoning.effort echoes high, so medium is a tier that has to be asked for and lands below the out-of-the-box run. *** THE DIAL IS MONOTONIC ON SCORE ACROSS FOUR RUNGS *** 9 at medium, 19 at high, 20 at xhigh, 33 at max. Four first-party rows, one serving path, model and prompt and repos held fixed. No other model on this board has four bench-measured tiers. *** AND THE VALUE CURVE IS THE WORST THIS BOARD HAS SEEN *** Cost per strict fix runs 1 dollar 11 at medium, 57 cents at high, 78 cents at xhigh, 60 cents at max. THE BOTTOM RUNG IS THE WORST BUY BY A FACTOR OF TWO. medium is not a cheap tier that buys less - it costs 10.02 dollars against high's 10.81, which is 93 cents on the dollar, and returns 9 points against 19. Nearly the same money for less than half the result. *** ON REPO 2 IT IS WORSE THAN NEARLY-THE-SAME: IT COSTS MORE IN ABSOLUTE DOLLARS *** 5.22 against high's 3.98, a 31 percent premium, for 5 of 60 against 11 of 60. More money, less than half the score, one notch of one field between the two runs. *** REASONING VOLUME DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT, WHICH IS THE FINDING FOR ANYONE PROBING A DIAL *** Total reasoning tokens are 187,162 here against 178,300 at high - medium is HIGHER, not lower. Per leg the direction is not even consistent: repo 1 down 5 percent, repo 2 up 24 percent, while the score halves on both. A token-volume probe pointed at this pair would have reported no degradation, or an improvement. Whatever the tier changed, it is not how much the model thought. *** IT RESOLVES A PREDICTION REGISTERED IN WRITING BEFORE THE RUN *** The magnitude probe was falsified twice on the top half of this dial. Its one surviving claim was that the bottom three tiers separate cleanly in TOKEN terms - medium's ceiling of 6,237 reasoning tokens per call below high's floor of 7,824 - and that claim had never been tested against score. The arm was launched with both outcomes written down: near 19 would mean a disjoint token band predicts nothing, well below 19 would mean the dial moves score at the bottom and high/xhigh is a dead zone in the middle. 9 is well below 19. The second branch stands. Honesty profile: 4 of 45 on repo 1 and 5 of 60 on repo 2, TWO partials on each, ONE claimed-only in total, and 8 genuine unplanted extras against high's 10 - the same band. The claim record does not degrade with the tier.
