The first-party row is a mean of 15, 13 and 17; this is a single 8 with no measured spread of its own, so the gap between them is known far less precisely than two numbers side by side suggest. billing=REAL BILL, and the control's is NOT: this figure is a credits delta read from OpenRouter before and after each leg, while the first-party row is a token-count estimate at list rates, because that endpoint publishes no usage API. A bill and an estimate are not the same kind of number and the 2.11-against-5.55 comparison should be read with that in mind. Token counts are NOT quoted for this row at all: for an arm behind a shim the local CLI invents them from an Anthropic price table, so they describe nothing real. EFFORT IS NOT ASSERTED. The first-party sibling runs at a MEASURED xhigh - that endpoint implements the effort field and rejects invalid values. OpenRouter does not implement it, so no tier was requested and none is claimed; the model reasons on its own regardless. THE CONTEXT DECLARATION OVERSTATES THE HOST, DELIBERATELY AND VISIBLY: the arm declares a 1M window to the CLI because through a shim that is the ONLY override the CLI accepts - every numeric alternative is passed through verbatim and rejected upstream as an unknown model id. This host's real ceiling is 262,144. The declaration never reaches the wire and only sets the CLI's local compaction arithmetic; the run peaked at about 203K, some 59K under the real ceiling. The earlier configuration, which accepted the CLI's 200K fallback instead, compacted 43 times on repo 1 and was killed outright on repo 2 - that attempt is void and is not this row.

The same Qwen3.8-27B weights as the first-party row, served at fp8 by a THIRD-PARTY host and reached through an aggregator - OpenRouter, pinned to one provider with fallbacks off, because OpenRouter lists 16 hosts for this slug at three different precisions (10 fp8, one bf16, one fp4) and an unpinned run blends across them per request and measures nothing. 8 of 105, against 15.0 for the first-party n=3 mean of the same model. That is below the WHOLE measured range of the control, whose three runs scored 15, 13 and 17. *** DO NOT READ THIS AS THE PRICE OF fp8 QUANTIZATION *** Two things changed together and this pair isolates neither: the PRECISION went from native to fp8, and the ROUTE went from Alibaba's own endpoint to an aggregator plus a third-party host. The same trap the Muse Spark OpenRouter-vs-first-party pair hit. A run that would separate them - this host at native precision, or Alibaba's endpoint at fp8 - does not exist and cannot be bought here, because neither party serves the other half. *** AND THE TWO ROWS DID NOT RUN IN THE SAME CONTEXT REGIME *** This is the more specific reason the gap is not clean. Through a shim the CLI cannot learn the upstream window, so this arm compacted 3 times on repo 1 and 2 times on repo 2; the first-party control compacted ZERO times on either repo and peaked at about 27K. Compaction throws context away mid-run, so part of this gap is a harness difference rather than a model difference. It is stated rather than corrected because nothing here can separate the two. WHAT IT IS GOOD AT: it is CHEAPER and no slower - 2.11 dollars against the control's 5.55, and 59.0 minutes against 56.8. And it did not over-claim: ZERO claimed-only fixes on either repo, so every fix it reported, the blind judge confirmed. It found 2 unplanted defects nobody had seeded, one per repo.

## gpt-oss-20b

**0 / 105** · repo 1 0/45 · repo 2 0/60 · 0 unplanted · 3.8 min · $0.11 bill · `default` · Claude Code / OpenRouter · single run · 2026-09-12

**Superseded.** Superseded 2026-09-15 by the n=3 MEAN row (0.0/105, range 0 - the mean confirms the single run exactly, six legs for six legs of zero). Nothing here is retracted: the 0 happened and this is its receipt. Kept rather than deleted because a supersession is only legible next to the row it supersedes.

The second ZERO, and the more instructive of the pair, because it did not run out of anything - it decided it was finished. On repo 1 it spent 55 seconds and 18 shell calls and then reported that all the public APIs were exercised by the suite, that type checking and the tests passed, and that no bugs were located that require a code change. The prompt it was given says in as many words that a number of subtle bugs have been deliberately planted, that the suite passes anyway, and that some tests which would have caught a planted bug were neutralised when it was planted. It used a green suite as proof of a clean repo after being told that a green suite proves nothing. On repo 2 it filed a report and scored zero on it: 0 of 60, no partials, and - the one thing to its credit - zero claimed-only, so it did not invent fixes it had not made. The whole arm cost eleven cents. *** THIS IS THE THIRD ATTEMPT AT THIS ARM AND THE FIRST CLEAN ONE, WHICH THE ROW WOULD BE DISHONEST WITHOUT *** Pass 1 ran before the harness carried any context declaration. Pass 2 ran with --autocompact 100000 on it, a flag since measured INERT on both routes by two controlled pairs - so whatever pass 2 was, it was not the flag doing it. Both passes were discarded and their rows dropped before this one ran. Pass 3 carries no flag and it is mechanically clean on both legs: terminal_reason=completed, zero auto-compactions, no breaker, no truncation, no denied tool call. That matters because this board voids a leg whose ENVIRONMENT failed and scores a leg whose MODEL failed, and the runner checks the transcript to tell them apart rather than guessing from an exit code. Here it counted successful tool calls and zero permission denials on every leg. The environment was fine. *** EFFORT IS THE PROVIDER DEFAULT AND THE ROUTE IS PROBED, NOT ASSUMED *** The arm carries no --effort flag, and that is a weaker claim than it sounds: a logging pass-through measured an UNFLAGGED Claude Code putting output_config.effort=xhigh on every main call, so there is no send-nothing mode. OpenRouter does not implement output_config, so on this route the tier is dropped in transit and what runs is Groq's own default - which is what makes default honest here and would not make it honest on a first-party endpoint. Receipts: effort-dial-probes/20260912-claude-code-wire-default-effort.txt and effort-dial-probes/20260912-gptoss20b-groq-openrouter.txt. OPEN WEIGHTS ON SOMEONE ELSE'S HARDWARE: OpenAI-released weights served by Groq, pinned with allow_fallbacks=false. More than twenty hosts serve these slugs and OpenRouter load-balances per request, so an unpinned run measures a blend rather than a model. Groq is the pin because it is the one host serving BOTH sizes, so this row and its sibling differ by parameter count and by nothing else. Cost is a REAL OpenRouter credits delta, not an estimate. WHAT A ZERO IS AND IS NOT: it is not a crash and it is not a harness failure. Both legs completed, the environment was verified healthy from the transcript, and the judge read a real submission on repo 2. This model was asked to find planted bugs in two repositories and found none of the 105.

Judged on the diff it found none of the 60 planted bugs; it did find one genuine issue nobody planted, plus one cosmetic. So the failure is not that it cannot read code.
